# Known Problems in Loft

Known bugs, unimplemented features, and limitations in the loft
language and interpreter.  Each entry records the symptom, workaround, and
recommended fix path.

Completed fixes are removed — history lives in git and `CHANGELOG.md`.

**Before opening a new issue here, check
[DESIGN_DECISIONS.md](DESIGN_DECISIONS.md)** — the closed-by-decision
register holds items explicitly evaluated and declined (C3 / C38 /
C54.D / …).  If your symptom maps onto one of those, the fix is to
produce new evidence (reproducer, incident, measurement) on the
existing entry, not re-open it as a bug.

## 🔴 Currently Open (fast index)

**At-a-glance list of every open P-issue.**  Each row jumps to the
full entry in the Quick-Reference table below.  Keep this in sync
with the table — `tests/doc_hygiene.rs::problems_open_index_matches_quickref`
asserts a row appears here iff the table row's severity column
contains `(open)`.  Run `make problems` for the same list from
the command line.

| # | Severity | One-liner |
|---|---|---|
| [@P392](#open-issues--quick-reference) | 🟢 FIXED 2026-06-01 (Low — parser silently mis-resolved typed-local declaration when LHS collides with stdlib fn name) | `now: vector<boolean> = self.keys;` parsed as `error: Expect token ;` at the `:`.  Root cause (NOT the originally hypothesized RHS-grammar restriction): `now` collided with the stdlib function `now()` (`default/02_files.loft:421`), the function-ref fallback in `parse_var` (`src/parser/objects.rs:297`) consumed the identifier, and the guard at line 320 only checked `=` (un-annotated assignment) — for the typed-local form the lexer was parked on `:` so the guard silently passed.  `parse_var` then emitted a `Value::Int` function-ref; back in `parse_assign` the typed-annotation arm failed to match, the `:` was never consumed, and the user saw a confusing `Expect token ;`.  Same root cause as P335 but in the opposite direction (P335: `n_<name>` user identifier alias; P392: typed-local form collision).  **Fix:** widened the guard at `src/parser/objects.rs:320` to also fire on `:` (the typed-local position), giving the same clear `Cannot redefine function '<name>' as a variable` diagnostic as the un-annotated form.  Regression: `tests/scripts/repro_p390.loft` (renumbered to repro_p392 if the slice-assign bug below keeps P390).  Originally filed as @P390; renumbered when the older slice-assign @P390 row was discovered. |
| [@P385](#open-issues--quick-reference) | 🟢 FIXED 2026-05-29 (parser type-inference asymmetry) | `if X { v[i] ?? null } else { null }` returning `text` failed with `error: expected text, got null on else` because the `OpConv*FromNull` lookup in `src/parser/mod.rs::convert` used `==` (exact-eq including dep vectors) to match `OpConvTextFromNull.returned` (= `Type::Text(vec![])`) against the target `Type::Text(deps_from_v_index)`.  Fixed in commit `4a7d716` by swapping the two `==` checks (lines 992 + 999) to `is_equal()` — `Type::is_equal` at `src/data.rs:830` already treats any `Type::Text(_), Type::Text(_)` pair as structurally equal.  Integer-bound narrowing (separate, earlier code path) is unaffected.  Regression: `tests/scripts/159-p385-text-nullable-else-null.loft` (joint with @P386). |
| [@P377](#open-issues--quick-reference) | 🟢 FIXED 2026-05-28 (`p377-fix`) — both LEAK (S1) and CORRUPTION (S2) | **LEAK class — FIXED (S1):** parse-time NRVO in `src/parser/control.rs::block_result` (helper `nrvo_collapse_tail_set`).  When the body's penultimate is `Set(cv, Call(fn_nr, […, __ref_X, …]))` and the tail is `Var(cv)`, substitute `__ref_X → cv` in the inner Call's hidden-buffer slot — inner Call writes directly into the outer fn's buffer; no intermediate store; no leak.  Regression `139-p377-libfree-leak.loft` runs in `loft_suite`.  **CORRUPTION class — FIXED (S2):** narrow codegen gate in `src/state/codegen.rs:1367-1375`.  The pre-Set `OpFreeRef` on `Set(v, Call(fn, args))` is now skipped when `args` contains `Var(v)` — i.e., when S1's substitution made the inner Call write directly into v's slot.  In that shape, freeing v's old contents would destroy what the Call is about to write into (it's the same store).  For every other shape (struct-literal RHS, non-S1 calls, calls whose hidden buffer isn't v), the load-bearing pre-Set FreeRef stays intact — `130-gridmesh-crystal-equiv.loft` and the moros_* library suite remain green.  Together S1+S2 form a complete fix at the parse + codegen layer; no runtime change needed.  Retires Paths A/B/C/D from the way-forward matrix for the intermediate-local class.  Regression `140-p377-corruption-oracle.loft` runs in `loft_suite`.  Native always passed; both backends now uniform.  Dryopea-surfaced |
| [@P382](#open-issues--quick-reference) | 🟢 FIXED 2026-05-28 (`p377-fix` branch) | `loft --interpret <file>` (the wrapper-synth path for files without `main()`) ran ALL `test_*()` fns in a single shared `State`, so per-call store leaks from the @P377 family (`map_get_hex(m: Map, …) -> Hex { return chunk.ck_hexes[idx]; }` deep-slice-borrow shape in `lib/moros_map`) accumulated until the next test's JSON cast (`cast_vector_from_text`, op 192) SIGSEGV'd.  Fix: replace the wrapper synthesis at `src/main.rs:3096-3147` with a per-test fresh-State loop (mirror of `src/test_runner.rs:997`'s `clean_data.clone() + State::new(clean_db.clone())` pattern; ALSO reset `clean_db.max = 0` because `Stores::clone` preserves `max` while clearing `free_bits`, so `find_free_slot` would otherwise return the stale max and OOB the freshly-empty `allocations` Vec on `State::new`'s initial `db.database(1000)`). |
| [@P381](#open-issues--quick-reference) | 🟢 FIXED 2026-05-28 (`p377-fix` branch) | `loft --interpret <file>` panicked when the file had no `main()` and only `test_*()` functions — the wrapper-synthesis at `src/main.rs:3135` called `compile::byte_code` a SECOND time over the same `State`/`Data` to compile a freshly-parsed `fn main()` wrapper, but the first `byte_code` call (line 2198) had already locked `CONST_STORE` ("compile.rs::compile (CONST_STORE init)").  The second call re-emitted bytecode for ALL user fns, and `Codegen::gen_text` (`src/state/codegen.rs:589`) tried to write a long string literal (≥256 chars) into the now-locked `CONST_STORE` via `set_str` → `assert!` at `src/store.rs:422`.  Native compilation tripped the same condition without the interpreter's assertion path and spin-looped instead.  Fix: refactored `compile::byte_code` to take a `start_d_nr` (new `byte_code_from` helper); the wrapper-synth site now passes the def count captured BEFORE the wrapper parse, so only the new wrapper `main()` is compiled and `build_const_vectors`/`CONST_STORE` lock are skipped (already done on the first call).  Verified: `target/release/loft --interpret lib/moros_sim/tests/persistence.loft` no longer panics; `loft --tests lib/moros_sim/tests/persistence.loft` passes 5/5; `native_library_suite` includes the file and passes. |
| [@P380](#open-issues--quick-reference) | 🟢 FIXED 2026-05-28 | building a **`vector<vector<single>>`** SIGSEGV'd on both backends (the dominant `93-vector-advanced.loft::test_vector_of_vectors` crash — reproduced in isolation, NOT cumulative).  A nested-vector element is a vector HANDLE, but the shallow element-type framing made `OpNewRecord` default-init it as the inner SCALAR; for 4-byte `single` the NaN sentinel (non-zero) was read by `OpCopyRecord`'s `remove_claims` as a garbage rec-id → wild `get_u32_raw` (8-byte int/float escaped: low-32 of their sentinels is 0).  Fix (`src/parser/vectors.rs`): zero the fresh handle slot before the copy (no type-system change, read path untouched).  Also fixed a stale `sizeof 8`→`16` expectation in the same file (integers are i64 now).  `93-vector-advanced.loft` re-enabled (un-ignored).  Regression: `tests/scripts/138-vector-of-vector-single.loft` (both backends) |
| [@P229](#open-issues--quick-reference) | 🟢 FIXED 2026-05-30 | Windows `--native` G2 (LNK1181 per-package link-search; `native_utils.rs`), G3 (concurrent `auto_build_native` re-resolution race; fixed by CI pre-build), G4-native (parallel verified on `windows-latest` CI run 26689698213).  All parts of @P229 closed.  CI run 26694041810: 2268/2268 passed, 0 failures on Windows.  `tests/codegen_emitter.rs::p310_graphics_vector_ffi_checks_clean` LNK1181 + G3 silent-skip branches removed; `p310` is the cross-platform regression guard.  Residual latent concern: @P388. || [@P321](#open-issues--quick-reference) | Low (native gate fully green; only browser-WASM of (c) left) | **7 parts (a–g); all native dimensions FIXED.** (c) `imaging`'s native direct-call ABI is now closed (`output_native_direct_call` forwards a `LoftStore` + marshals struct `Reference` args as `LoftRef`); imaging un-skipped, `native_library_suite` 53 green.  Only (c)'s browser-WASM path remains — route `load_png`/`save_png` to a JS host bridge, not a bundled Rust PNG codec. |

| 393 | **@P393** — **FIXED 2026-06-01 (Low — `11-vectors.loft` `LOFT_STORES=warn` false-positive warnings; verified NO leak).**  RESOLVED by the plan-57 **cluster II** fix (`ff8b0730`): the 2× literal-init store double-allocation was the dominant watermark contributor; removing it dropped 11-vectors's watermark from **44 → 26**, below the hardcoded `active>30` warn floor, so the ~14 `possible leak` warnings are GONE.  Residual benign watermark (clusters I scope-end-pinning + III reassignment-leak — both exit-safe, no leak) is O(locals) but below threshold; left as a design call in [`plans/2-vector-store-watermark/`](plans/2-vector-store-watermark/README.md).  *Historical:*  `LOFT_STORES=warn loft --tests tests/scripts/11-vectors.loft` had emitted ~14 `possible leak at alloc #N` warnings as the active-store count climbed to ~44 (interp) / ~42 (native).  **→ Investigation: [`plans/2-vector-store-watermark/`](plans/2-vector-store-watermark/README.md) — Stage A complete (9 probes, both backends, full trace analysis).**  Diagnosis corrected during Stage A: 11-vectors has **0** `test_*` fns (it is one `main()` with ~22 named vector locals — NOT subtests); the `LOFT_STORES=log` event trace is `aaaa…(44 allocs)…ffff…(42 frees)` with **zero interleaving**, so every store frees in one burst at function-scope exit → **verified no leak**; the `possible leak` warning is a false positive (heuristic floor `active>30`, `src/database/allocation.rs:142`, tripped by a legitimately-long function).  Two verified mechanisms: **cluster I** — named store-backed locals free at scope-end, not last-use (a 35-local probe holds 35; a 20-iter loop reuses ONE store); **cluster II** — `a=[literal]` allocates 2 scope-pinned stores (literal init-temp not reused), doubling the watermark (typed and untyped identical → not the annotation).  Unbound transient temps (slice-in-format) correctly free at statement-end → not the cause.  **Cluster III** (`v = [new]` reassignment does not free the old store) added 2026-06-01 via edge probes.  Aliasing/fix-safety resolved: loft vectors are copy-semantics on expression-assignment (`b = a[..]`; `b[0]=99` leaves `a[0]==1`), so dead-store freeing is safe; only `&vector` params alias.  **Repro:** `LOFT_STORES=warn ./target/release/loft --tests tests/scripts/11-vectors.loft`; trace `LOFT_STORES=log … --interpret …`.  **Fix path:** design call (raise heuristic floor [XS] vs literal-temp reuse [cluster II, S] vs last-use/overwrite-free [clusters I+III, M]).  **Two orthogonal codegen bugs surfaced during the edge probing and FILED separately** (different subsystem; do not fold here): @P394 (`b = a` new-local copy → no slot) and @P395 (`(v+[x]).len()` concat-temp in assignment → 8-byte stack drift) — both since FIXED. | 🟢 FIXED 2026-06-01 (via cluster II `ff8b0730`) | n/a — warnings gone; residual benign watermark (clusters I/III) tracked in plan-57. |
| 394 | **@P394** — **FIXED 2026-06-01 (High — codegen panic / hang / silent corruption; both backends).**  Declaring a NEW local from a bare vector variable — `fn main() { a = [1,2,3]; b = a; }` — panicked `Incorrect var b[65535] versus N` at `src/state/codegen.rs:2669` (the LHS `b` landed on slot `u16::MAX`, never allocated a real stack slot); with a trailing `b += [4]` it compiled but `b` was empty + the source corrupted (read-back HANG).  Triggered ONLY by a bare local/param vector-var RHS (expression / field / element / call / slice RHS all worked).  **Root cause:** the standard type-inference copied the RHS var's store dep onto `b` (an ALIAS), and the @P292 Clear+Append branch was gated on `uses(var) > 0` so a first assignment fell through to the alias path with no own store/slot.  **FIXED (`src/parser/expressions.rs`):** the bare-vector-Var branch now strips the RHS var's dep from `b` (breaking the alias via `make_independent`), then gives `b` its OWN store via `insert_new` on first assignment (or clears the existing store on reassignment) and `OpAppendVector` deep-copies — vectors are copy-semantics so `b` is independent of `a`.  Regression: `tests/scripts/167-p394-vector-var-copy.loft` (10 fns: copy / independence / `+=` / typed / untyped / text / float / reassign / chained — both backends).  Full suite green (3 native failures are the pre-existing `libring.rlib`-missing environment issue).  Remaining (separate, pre-existing): nested `vector<vector<T>>` var-copy is shallow — the same OpAppendVector deep-copy gap the reassign + slice paths have (no longer crashes; matches reassign behaviour). | 🟢 FIXED 2026-06-01 | n/a — fixed. |
| 395 | **@P395** — **FIXED 2026-06-01 (High — silent wrong value escalating to codegen panic; both backends).**  A `.method()` on an inline vector CONCATENATION receiver, in an assignment RHS — `x = (v + [9]).len();` / `acc += (v + [9]).len();` — silently miscompiled: it yielded a GARBAGE value (len 4 → `8589934592`) and, repeated, a `codegen.rs:2669` "+8 drift" panic.  **Root cause:** the inner concat inherited `orig_var` (the outer LHS `w`, typed `integer` — the `.len()` result) from the live `val` threaded through `parse_single`'s `(` branch, so it reused `w` as its vector accumulator, lowering to a void `Value::Insert` (no stack value); `.len()` then read a misaligned slot.  Inline vector concat consumed by a call is ALREADY a designed compile error (P103; `102-expected-errors.loft` pins `vec_sum([1,2]+[3])`); @P395 was a HOLE — the method-receiver shape bypassed the guard.  **FIXED (`src/parser/vectors.rs`, the `(` parens branch):** after the parenthesised expression, if a trailing `.` follows and the result is a vector-typed `Value::Insert`, emit the same clean P103 error.  Trailing-`.` discriminator is pass-stable and absent for the must-keep cases (`v = a + c`, `b = a + a`, `b = a + []`, `v = (a + c)`, `v = v + [9]` all keep their reuse path).  The `[index]` path already errors (not corrupts) and is left unchanged.  Regression: `102-expected-errors.loft::test_vector_concat_method_receiver` (@EXPECT_ERROR).  **Workaround (unchanged):** bind the concat first — `w = v + [9]; w.len()`. | 🟢 FIXED 2026-06-01 | n/a — fixed (clean compile error). |
| 392 | **@P392** — **FIXED 2026-06-01 (Low — parser silently mis-resolved typed-local declaration when LHS collides with stdlib fn name).**  `now: vector<boolean> = self.keys;` parsed as `error: Expect token ;` at the `:`.  Root cause: `now` collided with the stdlib function `now()` (`default/02_files.loft:421`); the function-ref fallback in `parse_var` (`src/parser/objects.rs:297`) consumed the identifier; the guard at line 320 only checked `=` (un-annotated assignment) so the typed-local form silently produced a `Value::Int` function-ref, the `:` was never consumed, and the user saw a confusing `Expect token ;`.  Same family as @P335 but in the opposite direction.  **Fix:** widened the guard at `src/parser/objects.rs:320` to ALSO fire on `:` and recover by binding the name as a Var (so `parse_assign`'s typed-annotation arm consumes the `:` cleanly).  Both un-annotated and typed-local now emit exactly ONE diagnostic.  Regression: `tests/scripts/repro_p392.loft`.  Originally filed as @P390; renumbered when the older slice-assign @P390 row was discovered (the row immediately below). | 🟢 FIXED 2026-06-01 | n/a — fixed. |
| 390 | **@P390** — **FIXED 2026-06-01 (Medium — self-slice-assign drops element values).**  `v = v[a..b]` (slice-assign of a vector back onto itself) preserves the slice's LENGTH but the resulting `v` reads as null at every index — element values are lost.  The non-aliased form `t = v[a..b]; v = t` works correctly.  **Repro (4 lines):** `fn main() { v: vector<integer> = [10,20,30,40]; v = v[0..2]; println("{v[0] ?? -1}, {v[1] ?? -1}"); }` → prints `-1, -1`; with the temp-local rewrite prints `10, 20`.  Surfaces across `vector<integer>` + `vector<float>` + `vector<text>` under `--interpret` (native codegen rejects the program with E0308 before reaching the issue).  Diagnosis (hypothesis): the codegen evaluates the RHS slice into a temp buffer, then assigns into the LHS storage IN-PLACE, overwriting the source bytes during the copy — or the slice IR aliases the source vector's storage and the assignment truncates length before reading.  **Surfaced** 2026-05-31 during @PLN80 5b graphics warning sweep — `draw_bezier`'s stack rewrite to `bz_sx = bz_sx[0..bz_top]` broke the bezier draw end (test_bezier_curve assertion).  **Workaround used in lib/graphics/src/graphics.loft:** introduce a temp local (`bz_tx = bz_sx[0..bz_top]; bz_sx = bz_tx`).  **Fix path:** inspect `OpSliceVector` codegen in `src/parser/fields.rs` / `src/fill.rs` — likely emit the slice into a new heap vector then swap pointers, or copy backward when LHS == source.  Regression-pin candidate: `tests/scripts/<NN>-p390-self-slice-assign.loft` checking the 4-line repro across all element types.  **FIXED 2026-06-01 (`src/parser/expressions.rs`):** the diagnosis was confirmed — `materialize_iterator` emitted `OpClearVector(var)` BEFORE the loop read `v[i]` from the SAME record (length preserved, values read null).  When the slice source IS the assignment target (detected via the new read-only `ir_mentions_var` walker, which mirrors `replace_var_in_ir`'s variant coverage), the slice now materialises into a temp local FIRST (the proven @P287 struct-field pattern), then `OpClearVector + OpAppendVector` on the destination — the temp breaks the alias.  Scoped to TRUE self-aliasing, so the non-aliased `t = v[a..b]` keeps the direct fast path and `+=` (which never clears) is untouched.  Works on BOTH backends — the temp-3-step IR compiles natively too (the earlier "native rejects with E0308" was a separate `?? <float-lit>` codegen issue, not the slice-assign).  Regression: `tests/scripts/166-p390-self-slice-assign.loft` (int/float/text self-slice + non-aliased control; passes interpret + native, leak-free). | 🟢 FIXED 2026-06-01 | n/a — fixed. |
| 388 | **@P388** — **FIXED 2026-06-01 (Low — `auto_build_native` unlocked concurrent re-resolution).**  `src/extensions.rs::auto_build_native` runs `cargo build --release --manifest-path <pkg>/native/Cargo.toml` with no `--locked`/`--frozen`, re-resolving the full dependency tree (169 packages) on every on-demand native build.  Under parallel nextest, concurrent re-resolution races with a standalone `loft --check --lib lib` rustc link → ureq/rustls rlibs transiently mid-rebuild/wrong-form → "required to be available in rlib format" (G3 of @P229).  CI pre-build (sequential build of all four native lib packages before the nextest step in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`) makes `auto_build_native` a no-op during the suite and fixes the CI symptom.  **Latent risk:** end users running parallel `loft --native`/`--check` invocations hit the same race; builds are also non-deterministic (may pick newer dep versions, e.g. "Adding png v0.17.16 (available: v0.18.1)").  Surfaced 2026-05-30 during the @P229 G3 hunt.  **Fix (landed 2026-06-01):** a cross-process advisory file lock (`std::fs::File::lock` on `<tempdir>/loft-native-build.lock`) now serialises every `auto_build_native` cargo build across processes, with the existing-artifact check re-run inside the lock so a waiter no-ops on an artifact a peer just built.  Eliminates the cross-process re-resolution race for both CI and end-user parallel `loft --native`/`--check` — every native-build path funnels through `auto_build_native` (`native_utils.rs:647`, `main.rs`, `parser/mod.rs`).  The `--locked`/committed-`Cargo.lock` route was rejected: the repo deliberately gitignores all `Cargo.lock`, and the crates.io-sourced native deps would drift against the actively-versioned loft-ffi crates.  Residual (benign): serial resolution is still non-deterministic across runs (version-picking), but no longer races.  Best-effort — if the lock file can't be opened/taken the build proceeds unserialised (no worse than before). | 🟢 FIXED 2026-06-01 | n/a — fixed (cross-process build lock). |
| 387 | **@P387** — **FIXED 2026-06-01 (monorepo fixture; Low — Windows test-fixture portability; incomplete @P333).**  `lib/hex_world/tests/hex_world.loft:104` still hardcodes `sv_path = "/tmp/loft_world_test.bin"` — the `/tmp/` literal @P333 swept out of `moros_render/geometry.loft` + `moros_sim/persistence.loft` but missed here.  On Windows `/tmp/` doesn't exist → `world_save` write fails (OS error 3, path not found).  **Not a current CI failure:** the `hex_world` lib links no graphics stack, so its native tests never hit the @P229 G2 LNK1181 link gate that masked the sibling fixtures, so hex_world.loft is not run-and-failing on the Windows leg today.  **Surfaced** 2026-05-30 while sweeping `/tmp/` fixtures during the @P229 G2 Windows work.  **Fix:** same as @P333 — CWD-relative filename (`loft_world_test.bin`) + `delete()` after use; line 124's `world_load("/tmp/loft_world_does_not_exist.bin")` is a deliberate miss-path probe (portable as-is, but move to a CWD-relative absent name for consistency).  Path bumped from `lib/world/tests/world.loft` 2026-06-01 (LAVITION W.1 rename).  **Fixed 2026-06-01** in `tests/fixtures/libs/hex_world/tests/hex_world.loft`: cwd-relative `loft_hex_world_test.bin` save name + `delete(sv_path)` cleanup + cwd-relative miss-probe; round-trip + cleanup verified on Linux (`loft test hex_world` green, no artifact left).  **Upstream tail:** the fixture is a pinned snapshot of `loft-libs-world @ hex_world-v0.1.0` (`scripts/sync-fixtures.sh`), whose source still carries `/tmp/` — fold the same change into that chunk repo before the next hex_world re-pin, or `sync-fixtures.sh` will re-introduce the literal. | 🟢 FIXED 2026-06-01 (monorepo fixture; upstream re-pin pending) | n/a — monorepo fixture fixed. |
| 386 | **@P386** — **FIXED 2026-05-29** (native codegen — text-nullable returns).  A function whose return type is `text` and whose body produces a text-nullable value (via `v[i] ?? null` or a bare `null` return) compiles cleanly under `--interpret` but fails native codegen with `error[E0308]: mismatched types — expected '&str', found 'Str'`.  Generated Rust at the failing line: `Str::new(loft::state::STRING_NULL)` (an OWNED `Str` allocated from the global null sentinel) in a context that expects a `&str` borrow.  **Surfaced 11×11 in `loft-libs-core::arguments` PR #2's `--native` step** (every test that compiled `arguments::get()` failed for the same reason).  **Minimal repro (7 lines):** `fn probe(v: vector<text>, idx: integer) -> text { if idx < len(v) { v[idx] ?? null } else { null } } fn main() { log_info("{probe([\"a\"], 5) ?? \"NULL\"}"); }` — interpreter passes (after @P385 fix); native fails with E0308 at the function's else-branch null materialisation.  **Diagnosis:** the text-nullable return value is materialised via `loft::state::STRING_NULL` (a `&'static str` constant), but the surrounding codegen wraps it in `Str::new(...)` (an owned wrapper) at the wrong layer.  Either the wrap should be removed where `&str` is expected, or the surrounding expression should borrow the wrapped form (`&Str::new(...)`).  **Fix (landed commit `8a44bb1`):** in `src/generation/emit.rs::output_if_inner`, detect when a text-result if-expression has a Block branch carrying the `??` `__ncc_*` skip_free pattern and wrap both arms in `.to_string()` (unifies on `String`); the outer Return routes the unified value through `stores.scratch.push(...)` so the surrounding `Str::new(stores.scratch.last().unwrap())` borrow is sound.  Sibling fix in `src/generation/calls.rs` adds `&*` deref for the same Block shape passed as a `&str` argument.  Regression: `tests/scripts/159-p385-text-nullable-else-null.loft` (joint with @P385) — re-enabled in `SCRIPTS_NATIVE_SKIP`.  Verified: `loft-libs-core::arguments::get()` now compiles + runs natively (11/11 tests pass with `LOFT_DENY_WARNINGS=1`). | 🟢 FIXED 2026-05-29 (text-nullable native returns now compile) | n/a — fixed. |

| 378 | **@P378** — **FIXED 2026-05-28 (both (a) interpret + (b) native)** (dryopea-surfaced; found while root-causing @P377).  **(a) Interpret — FIXED 2026-05-28:** `fn alloc_bag(seed) -> Bag { b = Bag{ items: [] }; b.items[seed] = …; b }` called in a loop (`bs = alloc_bag(i)`) **SIGSEGV'd** at op=219 (`set_keyed`) on the 2nd/3rd iteration's hash insert.  **Root cause (found via store-trace instrumentation):** `b` is a caller-provided `__ref_N` **return buffer** (ref_return promotes the returned local to a hidden buffer param).  In `main` the buffer is reserved ONCE at function scope (`__ref_1 = null` hoisted above the loop); each iteration `bs = alloc_bag(ci, __ref_1)` writes the Bag into the buffer and returns it, so **`bs` and `__ref_1` alias the same store**.  `OpFreeRef(bs)` at loop-body exit frees that store — leaving `__ref_1` a dangling reference to a now-free store.  Next iteration, `alloc_bag`'s own Item temp (`= null` → `find_free_slot`) is handed the freed store, while the stale buffer ref targets it too: the two `OpDatabase` calls collide on one record, so the keyed VALUE aliases the COLLECTION (`value == coll`, observed `coll=value=#2:1:8` in the trace) → self-referential keyed insert → wild pointer → SIGSEGV.  **FIX (landed, src/scopes.rs `witness_buffer`):** the existing `paired_witness` machinery only handled a witness that OUT-lives the buffer; it explicitly SKIPPED the inner-scoped-witness case (`v_scope > av_scope`).  Added the inverse: record witness→buffer and emit `OpFreeRefIfDistinct(bs, __ref_N)` for the witness's per-iteration free — a no-op while they alias (the buffer stays reserved across iterations and is freed once by its own function-exit `OpFreeRef`; the Item temp then gets a genuinely free slot, no collision), a real free in the fresh-store case.  Scope-safe for native because the buffer (outer/function) outlives the witness (inner), so the buffer's Rust `let` is still live where the witness free fires.  Regression: `tests/scripts/137-keyed-return-buffer-loop.loft` (both backends).  Verified: store trace now shows the buffer reserved once + freed once, Item temp clean per iteration; leak 34/34, wrap 49, native 6+3/6+3, full `find_problems` suite CLEAN.  **(b) Native — FIXED 2026-05-28 (native-only; @P236 family):** a function whose body is `if <cond with a ref-temp> { … } else { … }` returning a heap struct returned the **null DbRef sentinel** under `--native` → `src/keys.rs:251` `index out of bounds: len … index 65535` when the caller read it.  Narrowed precisely: the trigger is a **reference-returning call in the if-CONDITION** (`file(path).exists()`, `mk_ent(n).q == 0` — NOT `path == "x"` / `len(path) > 0`); the if/else alone (`if true {…}`) and a direct/local return are fine; the returned struct need NOT be keyed.  Mechanism: the condition's ref-temp is lifted to a `__lift_1 = call(); …` statement and its cleanup `OpFreeRef(__lift_1)` is emitted AFTER the tail if/else, so the if/else is no longer the syntactic last operator.  `returned_var` (`src/scopes.rs:1612`) takes the Block's LAST op (the `OpFreeRef`, → `u16::MAX`), so `block_result`'s `unify_if_branches_work_refs` never runs on the tail if/else and `scopes`/free_vars emits `Return(Null)`.  Interpret resolves `Return(Null)` via the bytecode-VM eval-stack-top (returns the if/else value — works); native emits a literal `return DbRef { store_nr: u16::MAX, … }`, dropping the computed value.  Same root family as @P236 (which fixed the simple tail-if/else case) — this is the variant where a condition-lift cleanup trails the tail.  **FIX (landed):** `scopes.rs::free_vars` — a new heap-return arm detects a tail `If` whose condition is a `__lift_N` Insert-preamble (and there are frees to run), and **preserves the value-emitting `Return(If(…))`**: it pulls the lift-preamble out, evaluates the boolean to a value-typed `__cond_N` temp, runs the frees (the lift's `OpFreeRef`, now BEFORE the return rather than unreachable-after), then emits `Return(If(Var(__cond_N), t, f))`.  (A first attempt — the obvious `__ret = if …; free; return __ret` save-to-temp — was tried and reverted: native voids the branches of `Set(var, If)` → `E0308`; only `Return(If)` value-emits them.)  Verified: the `if mk_ent(n).q == 0 { … } else { … }` / `if file(path).exists() { … }` shapes now return the real value on both backends.  Regression: `tests/scripts/repro_p378b_if_cond_lift_return.loft` (run by both `loft_suite` interpret and `native_scripts`); leak 34/34, wrap 49, native 6/6, full suite green.  **Consequence for @P377:** the deep-copy-with-free of a keyed-bearing fresh return cannot be enabled until (a) is fixed, so any @P377 fix must stay conservative for keyed-bearing returns.  **Repro (a):** single-file `alloc_bag` loop (interpret). | 🟢 FIXED (both (a) interpret keyed-return-buffer loop + (b) native if-cond-lift return) | none needed — fixed on both backends. |
| 377 | **@P377** — **FIXED 2026-05-28 (`p377-fix`)** — both classes closed via S1 (parse-time NRVO, commit `6909177e`) + S2 (narrow codegen gate, this entry).  **S2 (CORRUPTION fix):** `src/state/codegen.rs:1367-1375` now skips the pre-Set `OpFreeRef` when the RHS Call's args contain `Var(v)` (the LHS) — i.e., when S1's substitution made the inner Call write directly into v's existing slot.  Freeing v's old contents in that shape would destroy what the Call is about to write into.  For every other shape (struct-literal RHS, non-S1 calls, calls whose hidden buffer arg isn't v), the load-bearing pre-Set FreeRef stays — `130-gridmesh-crystal-equiv.loft` and the moros_* library suite stay green.  Detection condition: 5-line check (`Value::Call` RHS, iterate `args`, any arg `unspan()` == `Var(v)`).  Regression `140-p377-corruption-oracle.loft` runs in `loft_suite`; both backends now uniform.  S2 was found by extending the diagnostic plan's local-fix attempt: the broader `!is_argument(v)` / `!is_hidden_buffer(v)` gates traded 140-pass for 130-leak (slot-recycling invariant broke for shapes where S1 didn't substitute).  The narrower "S1-substituted call only" gate threads the needle.  **Historical context (pre-fix):**  **LEAK class — FIXED via parse-time NRVO (S1).**  `src/parser/control.rs::block_result` now invokes a new helper `nrvo_collapse_tail_set` immediately after `ref_return` at three sites (Vector / Reference-recovery / Reference-normal arms).  When the body's penultimate statement is `Set(cv, Call(fn_nr, […, __ref_X, …]))` and the tail is `Var(cv)` (or `Return(Var(cv))`, modulo `Span`) — AND `fn_nr` has a hidden Reference/Vector/struct-Enum attribute at index `i` whose corresponding `args[i]` is a parser-internal `Var(__ref_N)` / `Var(__rref_N)` — substitute `__ref_X → cv` in the inner Call's hidden-buffer slot via the existing `substitute_work_ref` helper.  Inner Call then writes directly into the outer fn's hidden buffer; no separate `__ref_X` store; no orphan; no leak.  `Set(cv, …)` becomes a same-store self-copy which existing `OpCopyRecord` codegen handles as a no-op (exercised today by every direct-return shape).  Six conjunctive preconditions (immediate-prev only; parser-internal name guard; hidden-attribute presence; first-pass guard; idempotency) keep the rewrite narrow.  Bails silently on any mismatch — no warnings, no errors.  Retires runtime-layer dep-tracking complexity (Paths A/B/C/D from the way-forward matrix) for this class.  Regression `139-p377-libfree-leak.loft` runs in `loft_suite`; the `cv = inner_call(...); cv` shape no longer leaks.  Side-benefit: the deep-slice-borrow shape (`return param.field[i]`) is unchanged by S1 (different penultimate shape; S1 declines to fire) — moros_* native library suite stays green (3/3 + 56 library scripts).  **CORRUPTION class — MECHANISM PINNED 2026-05-28 (diagnostic plan); FIX DEFERRED (M+).**  The corruption oracle (`tests/scripts/140-p377-corruption-oracle.loft`, ≥ 2 struct-param fns interleaved in a loop, `cv_q[0] = null(oob)` on iter 1) fails IDENTICALLY with S1 enabled or disabled (verified via `LOFT_DISABLE_S1` toggle).  Native passes the oracle.  **Root cause:** `src/state/codegen.rs:1367-1375` emits a pre-Set `OpFreeRef` on the LHS of `Set(v, Call(...))` when v's type is a `Reference`/`Enum(struct)` with empty deps.  For ref_return-promoted hidden buffer parameters (`become_argument(v)` + `attributes[idx].hidden = true`), this frees the CALLER's pre-allocated buffer mid-call.  **Why iter 0 works:** the freed slot is immediately recycled by the next allocation (first-fit, `src/database/allocation.rs:327::find_free_slot`) — caller's DbRef slot_nr is preserved, only contents change.  **Why iter 1 corrupts:** at end of iter 0, main's `__ref_1`/`__ref_2` slots hold DANGLING DbRefs to stores that were freed mid-call AND then re-allocated to OTHER variables (cv_p, cv_q) via first-fit; the iter-end free-loop frees those aliased stores, leaving main's __ref_1/__ref_2 truly dangling.  Iter 1's `p`/`q` allocations land in slots #2/#3 (first-fit), so main's stale __ref_1 now aliases `q`'s store; iter 1's render_p call passes the stale buffer DbRef, the callee's pre-Set FreeRef destroys q's store mid-call, downstream reads (cv_q.data[0]) corrupt.  **Diagnostic trace:** `/tmp/p377_full_trace.txt` — the smoking gun is at PC 6960 inside render_p (`FreeRef(ref(3,0,8)) ; - free #3` immediately after reading `var[268]=cv -> ref(3,0,8)` at PC 6957; cv is render_p's ref_return-promoted hidden buffer).  **Direct local fix attempted + reverted 2026-05-28:** added `&& !is_hidden_buffer` gate (via `attr_names` → `attributes[idx].hidden` lookup) on the pre-Set FreeRef condition at `:1370`.  140 PASSED — but 130 (`tests/scripts/130-gridmesh-crystal-equiv.loft`) introduced a leak (SegMesh×4).  Root of the leak: the pre-Set FreeRef was load-bearing for the slot-recycling invariant — skipping it leaves the old store allocated, AND the new Set's RHS allocates a FRESH slot (the old slot isn't free), orphaning the old store.  Confirmed via store-trace diff: baseline had `free #15; alloc #15` (same-slot recycle); post-fix had `alloc #16` (fresh slot, #15 orphaned).  Net: 140 ↔ 130 trade-off with no local fix.  **Fix scope: M+.**  Either (a) re-engineer the buffer-passing protocol so callees never free caller's buffer AND new content lands in caller's slot (in-place mutation, generalized beyond S1's immediate `cv = call(...); cv` shape), OR (b) full store refcount (Path C, `project_drop_store_refcount.md`).  S1 escapes for the immediate shape only — it avoids the inner allocation entirely, so there's no orphan.  Shapes where S1 doesn't fire (intermediate statements between Set and tail, e.g. `m = seg_mesh_new(); for ...; m` in `lib/audience_crystal/build_crystal_chunk`) remain exposed and would benefit from the same fix.  **Recommended next step:** open a dedicated plan slot (likely `plans/future/NN-p377-corruption/` or fold into the existing `project_drop_store_refcount.md` Path-C plan).  No further attempts on `p377-fix` branch — the local fix surface is exhausted.  **Pre-S1 history (kept for context):** under `--interpret`, a function that takes a **struct value parameter** AND returns a freshly-allocated store-backed struct via an intermediate local (`cv = canvas(...); cv`, e.g. `graphics::Canvas`) leaks **one store per call** — unbounded in a render loop (the dryopea editor leaks one ~960×720×4 Canvas every frame).  Scalar-param helpers and `--native` are clean.  **Root cause (found):** `ref_return` (`src/parser/control.rs`) promotes the returned local `cv` to a **hidden** return-buffer attribute; the deep-copy free gate `is_borrowed_view` (`src/state/codegen.rs`) uses `!returned.depend().is_empty()`, misreads the hidden-buffer dep as a borrowed view of a caller arg → the `0x8000` source-free is skipped → the fresh store is orphaned.  **TWO fix attempts, BOTH reverted as unsound:** (1) count only non-hidden deps as borrows → frees the source but causes **silent data corruption** (null reads) when ≥2 such fns coexist (freed slot reused while a live DbRef still aliases it).  (2) adopt-path — adopt a fresh (all-hidden-dep) return directly (no copy/free) → fixed the minimal cases BUT **crashed 8 library tests** (`moros_map`/`moros_render`/`moros_sim`): the all-hidden heuristic CANNOT distinguish a fresh return from a **deep borrowed view** — e.g. `map_get_hex(m: Map) -> Hex { … return chunk.ck_hexes[idx]; }` returns a slice of `m` but its recorded dep is a hidden work-ref, so it was wrongly adopted → use-after-free SIGSEGV.  **Real blocker:** the dep-inference does NOT record deep-slice borrows (`return param.field[i]`) as a dep on the param, so neither gate can reliably tell fresh-vs-borrowed.  **Proper fix needs:** either (a) make dep-inference record deep borrowed-view deps on the source param (then the non-hidden check is sound), or (b) a runtime ownership/refcount on the return store.  Both are M+ and touch the same machinery as @P378/@P380.  **Repro:** `/tmp/p_followups/p377_canvas_store_leak.loft` (needs graphics lib) — and now a **lib-free minimal repro** `/tmp/p_followups/p377_libfree_repro.loft` (inline `Canvas` struct + a two-level `render_struct(p: P) -> Canvas { cv = alloc_canvas(...); cv }`; leaks `Canvas×9`, while the identical `render_scalar(a: integer)` is clean).  **Mechanism confirmed via IR (2026-05-28):** the struct param `p` makes `has_ref_params == true` for `render_struct`, so the caller's `cv = render_struct(p)` takes the `gen_set_first_ref_call_copy` deep-copy path (NOT the adoption+`OpFreeRefIfDistinct` pairing that the scalar version gets); there `is_borrowed_view` skips the `0x8000` source-free and the callee's returned store orphans.  **CORRUPTION ORACLE built 2026-05-28 (`/tmp/p_followups/p377_corruption_oracle.loft`):** two struct-param fns interleaved in a loop with value-asserting reads turns the documented "silent null reads" mode into a deterministic assertion failure (`cv_q[0] = null(oob)` on iter 1).  Confirmed the corruption EXISTS ON THE CURRENT BUILD (interpret) before any fix attempt — it's not solely a leak; `has_hidden_ref` suppresses source-free, yet the corruption happens anyway, so the corruption root is NOT in source-free.  Native passes the oracle (matches the documented backend-asymmetry).  **WARNING for the next session:** do NOT simply re-try attempt (1) (non-hidden-dep gate) even though it now superficially passes — its failure mode is SILENT data corruption (null reads with ≥2 such fns), which the leak/wrap gates do NOT catch.  Any fix MUST gate against the oracle in addition to the standard suite.  Probable mechanism (not yet pinned): an OpDatabase / OpInitRef / find_free_slot interaction between iterations where freed slots get re-handed across the cv_p / cv_q / __ref_1 / __ref_2 vars — same family as the @P378(a) buffer-aliasing pattern, but in the deep-copy path which lacks the `witness_buffer` pairing.  **THIRD attempt tried + reverted 2026-05-28 (`p377-fix` branch, Stage 2 of the plan):** Candidate C (filter `is_borrowed_view` to non-hidden attribute deps in both `src/state/codegen.rs` paths AND `src/generation/dispatch.rs`) + Candidate D (drop `r.rec != 0` guard in `src/native.rs::n_protect_store_frees`/`n_unprotect_store_frees`).  **Result: REPRODUCES the documented attempt-2 library-crash mode.**  Gate output: 8 of 56 native library tests fail (`moros_map/tests/spawn.loft`, `moros_render/tests/geometry.loft`, `moros_sim/tests/{editor,persistence,picking,player_physics}.loft`, `moros_ui/tests/{editor_click,editor_panel}.loft`); wrap suite fails on `lib/audience_crystal/tests/01-editor-helpers.loft::main — Delete on locked store (rec=40)`; the persistence test SIGABRTs and the native binary spin-loops on the read-only-store assertion path.  **Candidate C alone fixes the libfree leak** but **introduces variant-10 corruption** (a 2-fn direct-return loop pattern that worked pre-fix now reads `null(oob)`).  **Candidate D alone breaks `lib/audience_crystal/tests/01-editor-helpers.loft`** with `Delete on locked store` (the always-protect of null-state buffers leaves a store free-protected through an unrelated delete site).  Net: no combination of C/D produces a green gate suite.  Stage 0 baseline (corruption oracle + libfree leak repro as `#[ignore]`'d regressions) is committed on the `p377-fix` branch for the next session.  **WAY-FORWARD MATRIX (2026-05-28 evaluation, in priority order):** **Path A — Bounded dep-inference enrichment (recommended fallback).** In `ref_return` (`src/parser/control.rs` + `src/parser/definitions.rs`), distinguish two reassignment shapes in the body: (i) `cv = inner_call(LOCAL_BUF); cv` → dep should be on the LOCAL (hidden, safe to source-free); (ii) `return inner_call(PARAM_BUF)` → dep should be on the PARAM (caller-visible, must NOT source-free).  Both currently record `["cv"]`/`["__ref_1"]` on the buffer-attribute name and the gate can't tell them apart.  Walking the return expression's data flow gives the right answer.  Then the existing Candidate C filter (`!attr.hidden`) becomes sound.  **Effort:** M+ (parser dataflow).  **Risk:** medium — the corruption oracle catches the silent mode, the moros_* gate catches the borrow-misclassification mode.  **Path B — Runtime detection at the OpCopyRecord source-free site (recommended FIRST attempt — narrowest).** At source-free decision, check if `data.store_nr` matches any of the call's ref-arg store_nrs **captured at protect-time** (instead of via the per-store `free_protected` bool which leaks state across unlock/init).  Requires a per-frame Vec capturing arg store_nrs in the protect bracket; the source-free check becomes membership lookup.  No dep-inference change.  **Effort:** S–M (~1 day).  **Risk:** low–medium — adds per-call state but the moros_* gate catches misclassifications and the oracle catches silent ones.  **Path C — Refcount on stores (out of scope for this branch).** Per `project_drop_store_refcount.md`, replace the current closure-capture refcount with a full store-ownership refcount.  Solves the @P377/@P378/@P380 family permanently.  **Effort:** L (1–2 weeks).  Its own plan.  **Path D — Fix free_protected state leakage (complement, not substitute).** Candidate D is *correct in principle* but breaks `audience_crystal` because some `Store::unlock`/`Store::init` paths clear `lock_origin` without clearing `free_protected`, leaving stores half-state (the rec=40 / empty `locked by:` symptom).  Fix the protect mechanism (recursive counter, OR consistent clearing on unlock/init).  **Effort:** S.  Land after Path A or B.  **RECOMMENDED SEQUENCE for next session:** Path B first (narrowest); if it doesn't generalize, fall back to Path A; defer Path C; Path D as a complement.  (@P381 was the prerequisite — FIXED on this branch 2026-05-28, see the @P381 row.)  **2026-05-28 PATH-B ATTEMPT (also reverted):** stack-based `protect_list: Vec<u16>` in `Stores` replacing the per-store `free_protected` bool, plus the Candidate-C filter — reproduced the same 8-moros_* library-crash mode + `Delete on locked store` in `audience_crystal` (the deep-slice-borrow `map_get_hex` shape returns a DbRef into a *transitively-owned* store inside the param, which the protect bracket can't protect since it only tracks top-level arg stores).  Architectural read of the failure: the dep-tracking / protect-bracket machinery is genuinely the wrong abstraction for the deep-slice-borrow case; a runtime ownership/refcount (Path C, per `project_drop_store_refcount.md`) is the right long-term direction.  **S1 LANDED 2026-05-28:** the LEAK class is closed at the PARSE layer (no runtime change), retiring Paths A/B/D for this class. But loft stores both an explicit `""` AND an *omitted* nullable text field as an allocated `""` (`u.name == null` is `false`), and a vector field carries no nullable flag.  **Fix:** text is null only when `text_nr == 0`; vectors are never null (always ≥ `[]`; `write_list` emits `[]` for a 0 handle). | 372 | **@P372** — **FIXED 2026-05-27** (dryopea-surfaced) — `text as Struct` HUNG forever (infinite loop, no error) when the struct's byte size exceeded 56 — e.g. 8+ `integer` fields, deterministically triggered by a `vector` field.  **Root cause:** `db_from_text` claimed a fixed `database(8)` (64-byte) record but wrote the struct at `pos: 8`, leaving only 56 bytes; a larger struct's tail field (a vector field's 4-byte handle) overflowed into the ADJACENT block's header and zeroed it, after which `Store::claim_scan` spun forever on the zero-sized block (`pos += abs(claim)` never advances — the `debug_assert_ne!` guard is compiled out in release). Gates: leak 34/34, wrap 49/49 (2 P377 known-broken ignored), native 6/6 + 4/4 suite, clippy/fmt/doc_hygiene clean.  **Note:** the `--interpret <file>` wrapper-synth path additionally surfaces a separate SIGSEGV (`op=192` at `lib/moros_map/src/types.loft:32:50`) when running the full persistence test under that path; `--tests` (the canonical library-test invocation) is clean. No type-system change (read path untouched); a no-op for the already-zero 8-byte cases.  Also corrected a **stale `test_format_object` expectation** in the same file (`sizeof 8`→`16`: a 2-`integer` struct is 16 bytes since integers became i64).  `93-vector-advanced.loft` **re-enabled** (removed from `ignored_scripts()`, `#[ignore]` wrapper deleted) — runs clean in the shared-State suite.  Regression: `tests/scripts/138-vector-of-vector-single.loft` (single crash case + int/float siblings, both backends).  Gates: leak 34/34, wrap 49/49 (0 ignored), native 6/6+4. | 🟢 FIXED | none needed. |

| 365 | **@P365** — **FIXED 2026-05-27** — an early `return [];` (an **empty vector literal** in a `return`) from a function whose return type is `vector<T>` was miscompiled on **both backends**.  **Minimal repro** (`/tmp/p_followups/p365_empty_vec_return.loft`): `fn sh(raw: text) -> vector<text> {{ if raw.len() == 0 {{ return []; }} raw.split('\n') }}` then `sh("")`.  **Observed:** `--native` — codegen emits `return ()` for the empty-vector path (`fn n_sh(...) -> DbRef {{ … return () }}`) → `error[E0308]: mismatched types`, native compilation aborts (compile-time, so it blocks *any* program with this shape).  `--interpret` — the empty branch yields a garbage vector handle → `panic: index out of bounds: the len is N but the index is <huge>` (`src/keys.rs:251`) when that branch is taken at runtime.  **Expected:** `return []` yields an empty `vector<T>` (len 0) — exactly what the **tail-position** empty already does (`"".split('\n')` correctly returns `[]`, and a binding `v: vector<T> = []` is fine).  **Root cause (one upstream typing gap, two divergent symptoms):** `parse_return` (`src/parser/control.rs`) did **not** thread the function's return type into the return expression (it called `self.expression`, never passing `r_type`).  An empty `[]` therefore typed as `Unknown` and `parse_vector`'s standalone-empty branch (`src/parser/vectors.rs`) emitted the lightweight `Insert([Null])` placeholder instead of a real vector store; native lowered that placeholder to `()`, the interpreter left a stale stack handle.  (A typed binding `v: vector<T> = []` and a non-empty `return [a]` both worked because they DID get an element type.)  **FIXED (parser, both backends):** (1) `parse_return` now threads the return **element type** — dep stripped (the fn's `[__ref_1]` dep must not transfer to the literal, or the buffer-copy arm's `!dep.contains(ref1_var)` guard would skip the copy) — into a `return [ … ]` literal; (2) `parse_vector` now **materialises a real empty vector store** for a *typed* standalone `[]` (routes through the normal zero-item construction path), so `return []` yields a valid len-0 vector with or without an `__ref_1` output buffer.  Regression: `tests/scripts/repro_p365.loft` (cross-backend — buffer / no-buffer / nested `vector<vector<T>>` / pre-bound-local / appendable-after-return).  **Surfaced** implementing @PLN32-lib P1 `split_headers` in `lib/web` (worked around there by dropping the redundant guard, since `"".split('\n')` already returns `[]`; the guard is still unnecessary). | ~~🟠 Med-High (both backends; native is a hard compile error)~~ FIXED | n/a (fixed) — was: avoid the early `return [];` (fall through to a tail / stdlib call returning `[]`, or return a pre-bound `e: vector<T> = []`). |
| 364 | **@P364** — **FIXED 2026-05-26 (native codegen; the remaining facet of training #12)** — A `Reference` local (e.g. a `JsonValue` from `json_parse`) whose ONLY use is in a function's **tail/return expression** was freed BEFORE that expression under `--native`, a use-after-free: `index out of bounds: the len is N but the index is 65535` (u16::MAX null sentinel) in `Stores::store` (`src/database/allocation.rs:461`) via `t_9JsonValue_field`.  **Minimal repro (no web/file):** `fn bearer() -> text {{ raw = "{{\"di_token\":\"xyz\"}}"; v = json_parse(raw); v.field("di_token").as_text() }}` → interpret `t=xyz`; native PANICKED.  **Root cause (NOT scope analysis — `scopes.rs` is correct):** for a text-returning tail `scopes.rs::free_vars` emits the safe B5-L3 form `[Set(__ret, Call), OpFreeRef(v), Return(__ret)]` (compute the text, THEN free `v`, THEN return).  But the native text-temp **collapse** in `src/generation/pre_eval.rs` (`[Set(__ret, Call), …, Return(Var(__ret))] → […, Return(Call)]`) HOISTS the `Call` from before the free-ops to the `Return` position AFTER them — and its only safety guard was "is `__ret` reused between?", never "do the intervening frees free a var the `Call` borrows?".  So `__ret = field(v).as_text(); OpFreeRef(v); return __ret` collapsed to `OpFreeRef(v); return field(v)…` → `field` reads `v` after it was freed + nulled.  The collapse's premise ("the inner Call borrows only a program-lifetime Store") is false when the Call borrows a scope-freed local.  **FIXED:** the collapse now also skips when the Set's `Call` mentions any var freed by a free-op between the Set and the Return (`free_op_var` + `value_mentions_var`); the non-collapsed path emits the sound scratch-copy return (`__ret` computed before the free; the returned `Str` points into program-lifetime `stores.scratch`, so no dangle either).  Interpret was always correct.  **Distinct from @P362** (auto-marshal i32 stack drift): #12 had two facets — interpret (@P362) and this native free-ordering one, both now FIXED. Surfaced re-validating training #12 (`garmin_read.loft`). | 362 | **@P362** — **FIXED 2026-05-26 (auto-marshal stack accounting)** — A program that `use`s `lib/web` and calls `web::http_get(...)` panicked with `index out of bounds: the len is 3 but the index is 8` in `Stores::clear` via `OpDatabase` (`src/database/allocation.rs:421`).  Symptom: the `HttpResponse` result variable `__ref_1` read `store_nr=8` though only 3 stores existed.  **Root cause (NOT store assignment — that was the symptom):** the library-native-call auto-marshal dispatcher (`src/extensions.rs::dispatch_call`) pushes a native fn's return value onto loft's 8-byte-slot stack.  Every integer-return arm widens the C `i32` to the full 8-byte slot via `stores.put::<i64>(stack, widen_int(...))` — **except** the `(&[Text,Text,Text,Text], Some(ArgT::I32))` arm (exactly `n_http_do("GET",url,"","") -> integer`), which used a bare `stores.put(stack, f(...))`.  Bare `put` infers `i32` and advances `stack.pos` by **4 bytes instead of 8**, so after the `http_do` call the frame is 4 bytes short; the very next op (`OpDatabase(__ref_1)`) reads `__ref_1`'s slot 4 bytes off, landing on adjacent stack memory (`store_nr=8`) → out-of-bounds clear → panic.  Pinned via instrumentation: `init_ref` wrote `__ref_1` at `stack_pos=84` (store_nr=2, valid); `OpDatabase` read it at `stack_pos=80` — a 4-byte drift across the `http_do` call.  Backend split is incidental (a small interpreter program panics immediately; native / larger programs only crash once the misalignment hits an out-of-range slot).  `json_parse` (Text→Ref) and non-web struct natives were fine — only the `Text×4→i32` arm was missed.  **FIXED:** that arm now uses `stores.put::<i64>(stack, widen_int(f(...)))` like its siblings.  Verified: interpret single `http_get` (was panic) → `status=200`; native single + 2-large-fetch+parse → all steps succeed; native/native_ext/native_loader + issues (680) suites green, clippy clean.  Surfaced in the training port (their #12) building the native Garmin reader. | 356 | **@P356** — **FIXED 2026-05-26 (both backends; [plans/finished/48-recoverable-oob-index](plans/finished/48-recoverable-oob-index/README.md))** — **Backend divergence on out-of-bounds vector index.**  `v: vector<integer> = []; x = v[0];` → the INTERPRETER **raises** (`error: index 0 out of bounds for length 0`) but `--native` returns **`null`** (no raise) — identical code, identical input, opposite behaviour.  [LOFT.md:1008](LOFT.md) documents `v[i]` as "null if out of bounds" (and the compiler warns "v[i] may produce null on out-of-bounds…"), so NATIVE matches the documented intent and the interpreter diverges — BUT the documented null behaviour is *dangerous*: in the training port an unexpectedly-empty wellness vector made `w.day[0]` null under `--native`, which then became a date-stepping `while`-loop bound → astronomical loop → 100% CPU **HANG**; the interpreter raised cleanly on the same input.  So the divergence is real and the "correct" semantics is a genuine **design decision** (fail-fast raise vs documented nullable) with broad impact — changing either backend touches every `v[i]` in the language.  Surfaced porting the training store engine.  Repro: `../personal/training/loft/bugs/loft_native_oob_null.loft` (native → `null`; `--interpret` → raises).  **Decision (2026-05-26, user sign-off): option (A′) — RECOVERABLE semantics on BOTH backends.**  Per project policy (runtime aborts for *reversible* faults belong only in opt-in debugging, not release runs — see [feedback_avoid_runtime_errors]), explicit `v[i]`/`s[i]` OOB should return the null sentinel + log a `Warn` and CONTINUE (matching native + the documented `??` idiom), with fail-fast available only via `LOFT_DEV_SOFT_HALT`.  NOT (B) fail-fast.  **Attempted + REVERTED 2026-05-26 — this is an M+ coordinated change, not a one-shot fix.**  The behaviour change itself is trivial (route `vec_get_or_raise` / `text_char_or_raise` + the `_runtime` peers through a new `raise_recoverable` that logs-and-continues unless `LOFT_DEV_SOFT_HALT`), and made both backends agree (`x=null`, exit 0).  But making OOB CONTINUE (instead of halt) exposes latent crashes that the halt previously masked: **(1)** the `??`/ncc lowering for a text-vector index (`tv[0] ?? null`) keeps the *raising* `OpGetText(OpGetVector(...))` (the `swap_to_nullable` helper in `src/parser/operators.rs:888-906` handles `OpGetInt/GetByte/GetShortRaw` wrappers but NOT `OpGetText`/other element getters), and the ncc materialises the result into a `text["dep"]` temp via `v_set`; **(2)** that temp is then scope-freed by `OpFreeText` → `string_mut(pos).shrink_to(0)` (`src/state/text.rs:307`), but the slot holds a VIEW/null (16-byte `Str`/sentinel), not an owned 24-byte `String` → reads a garbage capacity → SIGSEGV ("Tried to shrink to a larger capacity").  Surfaced via `tests/scripts/37-stress.loft:116` (`assert(!(tv[0] ?? null), …)` after `s#remove`-ing all elements) — a script DESIGNED to abort on OOB, so the abort-expecting scripts (37-stress + the leak-grandfather list: 06-structs / 11-vectors / 93-vector-advanced / 96-slot-assign / 07-vector / 16-parser / 15-lexer / 23-safety) also need their OOB-abort expectations revisited.  Plus the whole `tests/runtime_errors.rs` index suite + `tests/runtime_logging.rs` prod tests + `tests/error_messages` baselines `20/21_runtime_index_oob_*` encode the fail-fast contract and must flip to recoverable.  ****FIXED via [plans/finished/48-recoverable-oob-index](plans/finished/48-recoverable-oob-index/README.md)** — both backends now return the typed null sentinel + log a `Warn` + CONTINUE on OOB / negative `v[i]` / `s[i]`; `LOFT_DEV_SOFT_HALT` opts into fail-fast surfacing.  The fix had four parts: **(A)** `gen_if` branch-leveling — an expression `if {value} else null` (the `expr ?? null` ncc lowering `if bool(tmp) {tmp} else null`) now pushes a TYPED NULL on the false path via `emit_typed_null` instead of skipping to the join; without it the null branch left the value-sized slot unwritten so the runtime stack was short by `size(tp)` and the ncc temp's `OpFreeText` freed a mis-offset uninitialised slot → SIGSEGV (root cause was here, NOT the getters — the original `OpGetText` rec==0 guard hypothesis was wrong); **(B)** completed the `??`→nullable swap (`rewrite_outer_arith_to_nullable`, `src/parser/operators.rs`) to cover every element getter (`GetText`/`GetSingle`/`GetFloat`/`GetShort`/`GetEnum`/`GetCharacter`/`GetField`/`GetDbRef`) + a two-level `vector<boolean>` (`OpEqInt(OpGetByte(OpGetVector))`) hop, so `tv[i] ?? fb` over any element type routes to the nullable op; **(C)** added `State::raise_recoverable` / `Stores::raise_recoverable_runtime` (log-`Warn`-and-continue unless `LOFT_DEV_SOFT_HALT`) and routed `vec_get_or_raise` / `text_char_or_raise` + the `_runtime` peers through them; **(D)** flipped the `tests/runtime_errors.rs` index suite (recoverable + soft-halt surfacing), the 2 `tests/runtime_logging.rs` prod tests, and the `error_messages` baselines `20`/`21`.  Regression: `tests/scripts/repro_p356.loft` (empty/emptied vec `v[i]`, OOB, negative, text OOB, and the `tv[0] ?? null` crash case — all element types, both backends via `loft_suite` + `native_scripts`).  Validated: 37-stress (the script that crashed) now passes on both backends; full suite green.  Repro: `../personal/training/loft/bugs/loft_native_oob_null.loft` (native → `null`; `--interpret` → raises); minimal crash repro `tv: vector<text>=["a"]; for s in tv {{ s#remove }}; r = tv[0] ?? null` (interpreter SIGSEGV once OOB stops halting). | ~~Medium (two backends silently disagree; the null path can HANG)~~ FIXED | n/a (fixed) — both backends return null + log a `Warn` + continue; `v[i] ?? <fallback>` still works identically and `LOFT_DEV_SOFT_HALT` gives fail-fast. |
| 355 | **@P355** — **FIXED 2026-05-26 (both backends)** — `file(path).content()` (and ANY text over **10 MB**) silently returned `""` — surfaced in the `../personal/training` port reading a 38 MB `daily_wellness.json` (read len 0 → empty `json_parse` → downstream OOB/wrong result).  **Root cause:** `Str::str()` (`src/keys.rs`, the UNIVERSAL text-read accessor) had a `self.len > 10_000_000` guard that returned `""` — added in #140 as a SIGSEGV defence for the `stack_trace` introspection path (reading uninitialised stack slots), but it landed in the hot correctness accessor and silently truncated legitimately-large text (the file read itself succeeded — `read_to_string` returned the full 12 MB, then `len()` read it back as 0).  Measured cap was exactly 10 MiB (`10_485_760` failed, `10_000_000` passed = `> 10_000_000`).  **FIXED:** dropped the `len` cap from `str()` (kept the near-free null / low-pointer guards that catch an empty `String`'s dangling ptr); the fallible `try_str()` variant the introspection/trace dump actually uses (`src/state/debug.rs`) keeps its full guard set, so garbage-tolerance stays where it belongs.  Verified: 10.5 MB / 12 MB / 40 MB files now read fully on interp + native.  Regression: `src/keys.rs::p355_large_str` (12 MB round-trip + empty/null-ptr guard). | ~~Medium (silent data loss on any >10 MB text; bites data-processing / large-JSON)~~ FIXED | n/a (fixed) — was: keep files/texts under 10 MB. |
| 354 | **@P354** — **FIXED 2026-05-26 (interpreter; 9 sibling natives hardened)** — Two-or-more **sibling call arguments** that each produce a scratch-backed text (`JsonValue.as_text()`, `.to_json()`, `.kind()`, `Type.parse()` `#errors`, `json_errors()`, `source_dir()`) **aliased each other's return buffers** under `--interpret`: every argument collapsed toward the LAST call's value.  `fn f(o,k){ o.field(k).as_text().to_lowercase() } … show(f(o,"x"), f(o,"y"), f(o,"z"))` printed `[cc][cc][cc]` instead of `[aa][bb][cc]` (bare `.as_text()` corrupted even more wildly).  `--native` was CORRECT for `as_text` but had the SAME latent bug in `json_errors`/`parse_errors`.  Surfaced porting the training OSM gatherer: three `tag_str(tags, "surface"/"highway"/"tracktype")` sibling args made `highway` receive a fragment of `tracktype`.  **Root cause:** `n_as_text` (`src/native.rs`) and 8 sibling text-returning natives did `stores.scratch.clear(); stores.scratch.push(s); … Str::new(&stores.scratch[0])`.  Text values are `Str{ptr,len}` views into `Stores::scratch` (a `Vec<String>`).  The `clear()` **dropped every earlier scratch String** — including ones whose `Str` views were still sitting on the stack as already-evaluated sibling call arguments — and the freed heap buffers were immediately reused by the next push, so all siblings ended up pointing at the last value.  The canonical producers (`to_lowercase`, `t_9JsonValue_as_text` native, …) never clear; the 9 offenders had cargo-culted a `clear()` with no justifying comment.  **FIXED:** all 9 sites (7 in `src/native.rs` — `n_as_text`, `n_source_dir`, `i_parse_errors`, `n_json_errors`, `kind`, `n_to_json`, `n_to_json_pretty`; 2 in `src/codegen_runtime.rs` — `i_parse_errors`, `i_json_errors`) now `push` + return `Str::new(stores.scratch.last().unwrap())` without clearing; `OpClearScratch` (emitted at each source-line boundary) reclaims the buffer between statements.  Same family as @P329/@P243 (text-return buffer lifetime), one layer up (multiple sibling calls vs single tuple/return).  Regression: `tests/scripts/repro_p354.loft` (as_text().to_lowercase(), bare as_text(), and to_json() sibling triples; both backends via `loft_suite` + `native_scripts`). | ~~Medium (silent wrong text on the interpreter; common JSON-field-extraction shape)~~ FIXED | n/a (fixed) — was: hoist each call into its own local before the outer call. |
| 353 | **@P353** — **FIXED 2026-05-26 (native codegen)** — An EMPTY `vector<fn(...)>` literal failed to COMPILE under `--native`: `fs: vector<fn(integer)->integer> = []; print("{len(fs)}")` → `error[E0425]: cannot find value 't65' in this scope`.  The interpreter was always correct (`len=0`).  A NON-empty fn-ref vector literal (`[dbl]`) compiled + ran fine on both backends (what @P343's fix exercises), so the gap was specifically the empty-literal native codegen for `Type::Function` element content.  Surfaced while testing @P343's fn-ref for-loop fix against an empty input.  **Root cause:** the synthetic wrapper struct `main_vector<fn(integer) -> integer>` (type_id `t64`) has a field `vector` whose content is `int<0,false>` (`t65`, the fn-ref's d_nr-storage int) — but that narrow int is created as a SIDE-EFFECT of the wrapper's field registration at parse time, so its runtime type id (65) is HIGHER than the wrapper struct's (64).  The single-pass `known_type`-ordered emission loop (`src/generation/mod.rs`) flushed `bare_io` only when `bare_io[idx].0 < type_id`, so `t65` was emitted AFTER `t64`'s field referenced it → forward reference → E0425.  Every NORMAL wrapper (`main_vector<integer>`→t0, `<text>`→t5, `<JsonValue>`→t52) has content id BELOW its own id, so only this fn-ref→narrow-int inversion tripped.  **FIXED:** all `Stores::{int,byte,short,vector,…}` constructors are INTERNED (return the existing id by name; only the FIRST creation fixes the runtime id), so re-emitting a bare type is a harmless no-op.  `emit_field` now calls a new `flush_bare_through(w, bare_io, bare_emitted, content_id)` before each `db.vector(content)` — emitting the content bare type's creation in id order if it has not yet been emitted.  The pre-struct flush (`limit = type_id`) and a `bare_emitted: Vec<bool>` tracker replace the old `bare_idx` cursor + two duplicated inline match blocks (also de-duped into `write_bare_io`).  `BareIo` hoisted to module scope.  The JsonValue enum↔variant mutual-recursion cycle-break is preserved (its content ids are all below their referents, so the per-field flush is a no-op there) — verified `57-json` + `repro_p357` compile + run natively.  Regression: `tests/scripts/repro_p353.loft` (empty + seeded fn-ref vector literal; runs on both backends via `loft_suite` + `native_scripts`).  Repro: `/tmp/p_followups/loft_empty_fnvec_native.loft` (interp → `len=0`; `--native` → now `len=0` too). | ~~Low (native-only; empty fn-ref vector literal — rare shape)~~ FIXED | n/a (fixed) — was: seed the vector with at least one fn-ref, or build it incrementally on native. |

| 350 | **@P350** — **FIXED 2026-05-26 (the silent rlib-stomp half; asyncify half still warns by design)** — A **direct** `loft --html out.html prog.loft` silently produces a BROKEN bundle when `target/wasm32-unknown-unknown/release/libloft.rlib` is the `feature=wasm` (wasm-bindgen) variant — i.e. after a `make wasm` (wasm-pack) run, which writes the SAME path with an incompatible feature set (the @P337 "rlib stomp").  The resulting wasm imports `__wbindgen_placeholder__` (35+), which the embedded `loft-gl-wasm.js` glue does not provide → the page fails to instantiate (`Import … __wbindgen_placeholder__: module is not an object`).  `loft --html` currently only warns about a missing `wasm-opt`; it does NOT detect a stomped rlib.  The new integrity gate (`tools/check_html_bundle.mjs`) is wired into **`make game` only** — a bare `loft --html` has no guard, so the broken artifact ships unnoticed outside the Makefile path.  **Robust fix (one of):** (a) build the `--html` rlib in an ISOLATED `--target-dir` so `make wasm` and `--html` never share the path (eliminates the stomp at the source), or (b) have `loft --html` self-validate its emitted wasm (reject if any import module ≠ `loft_gl`/`loft_io`, or if no `asyncify_start_unwind` export) and abort with the exact rebuild command instead of writing a broken HTML.  Surfaced while diagnosing @P337; the doc + `make game` gate landed, but the direct-invocation hole remains.  See [WASM.md § The rlib-stomp hazard](WASM.md#the-rlib-stomp-hazard--eliminated-by-per-shape-isolation-pln100-slice-1).  **FIXED 2026-05-26 via option (b), import-module check inline:** `loft --html` now self-validates the emitted wasm BEFORE writing the HTML (`src/main.rs`, after `final_wasm` is read) — `native_utils::html_wasm_import_modules_ok` parses the wasm import section and HARD-ABORTS (exit 1, no HTML written) if any import module is not `loft_gl`/`loft_io`, printing the exact rebuild command (`cargo build --release --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --lib --no-default-features --features random`).  This closes the SILENT half — the stomped bundle that instantiates nowhere is now never written by the bare command, matching `tools/check_html_bundle.mjs`'s import check.  The parser is conservative (any shape it can't walk → `Ok`, never a false abort; verified against a real valid bundle which the JS gate also passes).  The asyncify/`wasm-opt`-missing half is deliberately NOT a hard abort: it already prints a loud warning and is *conditional* on whether the program frame-yields (a compute-only `--html` program is fine with no asyncify), so aborting it would false-reject valid compute bundles.  Unit tests: `src/native_utils.rs::p350_html_wasm_import_check` (accepts loft_gl/loft_io, rejects `__wbindgen_placeholder__`, no-imports ok, malformed→no-false-abort).  Robust fix (a) (isolated `--target-dir` to prevent the stomp at the source) remains a possible future hardening but is no longer needed for safety. | ~~Medium~~ FIXED (silent stomp now hard-aborts; asyncify still warns by design) | n/a (fixed for the stomp) — `loft --html` aborts with the rebuild command instead of writing a broken bundle.  Still ensure `wasm-opt` (binaryen) is installed for frame-yielding game bundles — `make doctor`. |

Fixed by rebuilding (`make wasm`).  **(2) JSON ecosystem not bundled** — `DEFAULT_FILES` in `src/wasm.rs` embedded `01_code`..`05_coroutine` but OMITTED `06_json.loft`, so `json_parse` resolved as `Unknown function` in-browser (the native fns were already compiled in — unconditional registration in `src/native.rs`, no wasm cfg-gate — only the loft-source declarations were missing).  Fixed by adding `06_json.loft` to `DEFAULT_FILES`.  **(3) runtime `file()` ignored VIRT_FS** — `State::get_file_text`'s `#[cfg(feature="wasm")]` branch (`src/state/io.rs`) read only via the JS `host_fs_read_text` bridge, which the browser playground does not provide → `file("x").content()` returned `""` even though `compile_and_run` populates `VIRT_FS` with every passed file (the LEXER already read source/lib files from `VIRT_FS` via `virt_fs_get`, but the runtime read did not).  So `json_parse(file(...).content())` got an empty string → `JNull` → `.field().as_number()` → `NaN`.  Fixed: the wasm branch now consults `crate::wasm::virt_fs_get(path)` FIRST, falling back to `host_fs_read_text` (a live-FS host with no VIRT_FS entry is unaffected).  **Verified under Node** (`initSync`+`compile_and_run`): `file("zd.json").content()` → `HELLO123` (was `""`); `json_parse(file).field("activities").item(0).field("duration_s").as_number()` → `3600` (was `NaN`), matching native.  **Remaining caveat (minor, not separately filed):** `compile_and_run`'s `run_pipeline` picks the compiled `main` as the alphabetically-FIRST non-default/non-lib file (`.min()`), so a passed data file whose name sorts BEFORE the program (e.g. `a_data.json` < `main.loft`) is mis-compiled as the program → confusing parse error.  Workaround: name data files to sort after the program (e.g. `zdata.json`), or the harness should pass the entry file as the lexicographically-smallest name. | ~~Medium~~ FIXED | n/a (fixed) — was: arrays-as-inline-strings worked; objects/files did not. |

| 347 | **@P347** — **FIXED 2026-05-26 (was native-only)** — An ORDERING comparison (`<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=`) between a `vector<text>` element and another text value failed to COMPILE under `--native`: `if days[i] >= start { … }` emits `(&var_start) <= (&*({…get_str…}))` — `&String` (the local) vs `&str` (the indexed element via `store.get_str`), which `PartialOrd` won't unify (`error[E0308]: mismatched types` / `error[E0277]: can't compare str with String`).
