| 346 | **@P346** — **FIXED 2026-05-26 (was interpreter-only)** — A string-interpolation expression used directly as an `if`-branch, over a vector-indexed value, inside a loop, **accumulated its text across iterations** under `--interpret` — silent wrong output.  `for i in 0..len(vs) { x = vs[i]; xj = if true { "{x}" } else { "null" }; out += "[{xj}]"; }` over `[2.5, 8.0, 1.0]` prints `[2.5][2.58][2.581]` (each row = concatenation of values 1..N) instead of `[2.5][8][1]`.  `--native` is CORRECT.  Trigger needs ALL of: (a) value from a vector index (`vs[i]`), (b) the `"{x}"` interpolation sitting *inside* an `if`-branch assigned to a var, (c) a loop — interpolating `x` directly (no if-wrap) or over a non-indexed local is fine, so the interpolation work-buffer for the if-branch result is never reset between iterations.  Repro: `/tmp/p_followups/loft_interp_accum_in_if.loft`.  Surfaced porting the training-app Phase-2 store engine.  **FIXED 2026-05-26**: the if-branch interpolation's work-buffer is promoted to the function's `&text` return slot (`RefVar(Text)`); its per-iteration reset is `Set(refvar, "")`, but `State::set_var` (`src/state/codegen.rs`) treated an empty-text assignment to a `RefVar(Text)` as a NO-OP (early return) — so the dereferenced buffer kept the previous iteration's content and the following `OpFormatStack*` APPENDED to it.  Fix: that case now emits `OpClearStackText` (deref-clear), matching the native path (an assignment IS a replace, not a no-op).  Regression `tests/scripts/repro_p346.loft` (float + int interpolation, both backends).  Verified no regression across issues/wrap/expressions/leak/format/strings suites. | ~~Medium~~ FIXED | n/a (fixed) — was: hoist the interpolation out of the if. |
| 345 | **@P345** — **FIXED 2026-05-26 (diagnostic; syntax intentionally unsupported)** — An explicitly-typed for-loop variable does not parse: `for i: integer in [1,2,3] { … }`.  This is **working as intended** — a loop variable's type is fully determined by the iterable's element type, so an annotation is always redundant.  Previously it produced a misleading 3-error cascade (`Expect token in` → `{` → `;`).  **Fix:** `parse_for` (`src/parser/collections.rs`) now detects a `:` after the loop var and emits ONE clear message — *"loop variable 'i' is type-inferred from the iterable — remove the ': <type>' annotation (write `for i in …`)"* — then recovers by skipping the (possibly compound, e.g. `vector<text>`) annotation up to `in` so no spurious cascade follows.  Verified for simple and compound annotations. | ~~Low~~ FIXED | n/a — drop the annotation: `for i in [1,2,3] { … }`. |
| 344 | **@P344** — **RESOLVED 2026-06-01 (accepted-as-intended; documented in CAVEATS.md § P344)** — Reusing a for-loop variable NAME across two loops in the same function fails when the element TYPE differs: `for i in [1,2,3] {…}` then `for i in ["a","b"] {…}` → `error: loop variable 'i' has type text but was previously used as integer`.  Same-NAME reuse works fine when the type is consistent (`[1,2,3]` then `[4,5,6]` → `1 2 3 4 5 6`), including across different functions — so the real constraint is per-name TYPE consistency, NOT name uniqueness.  Consequence of loft's per-function flat variable table (one slot + type per name; each `for` reuses the slot rather than opening a fresh scope).  Front-end error, identical on both backends.  Surfaced by the `../personal/trainer` app agent ("loop index variables cannot be the same for two subsequent loops" — actually only when the types differ).  Note: the loft-write skill (SKILL.md:551/705) over-states this as "loop variables must be unique across the entire file"; the accurate rule is "a reused loop-var name must keep a consistent type."  Repro: `/tmp/p_followups/loft_loopvar_type_conflict.loft`.  Fix direction: give each `for` body its own scope for the loop variable (fresh slot per loop), or document as intended.  **Fix design (2026-05-26):** The diagnostic is intentional (`parse_for_iter_setup`, `src/parser/collections.rs:1216-1236`) and guards a HARD invariant of loft's flat per-function variable table — `src/variables/mod.rs:89`: *"Variables might exist in multiple scopes but not with different types."*  A name resolves to ONE slot whose type is fixed for the whole function, so a second loop binding `i` to a different element type cannot reuse the slot.  **Two options. (A) Document-as-intended (recommended, XS):** the symptom has a zero-cost workaround (distinct names) and common shapes work; the only real defect is that the loft-write skill (`SKILL.md:551/705`) OVER-states the rule as "loop variables must be unique across the entire file" — correct it to the accurate rule "a reused loop-var name must keep a consistent type" and downgrade this row to a CAVEATS.md entry.  **(B) True per-loop scoping (M, deferred):** make the loop variable bind a FRESH `Variable` entry scoped to the loop body that shadows any prior binding of the same name, so `var(id)` resolves to the innermost binding while inside the body and pops back afterward — this lifts the one-type-per-name limitation generally, not just for loops, but it touches the core resolver (`Variables::var`/`set_scope`/scope-pop) and the slot-assignment liveness model, so it is a model change, not a local fix.  **Recommendation:** ship (A) now (fix the skill text + move to CAVEATS), and only pursue (B) if a consumer hits it where distinct names are genuinely awkward.  Regression for (A): keep `/tmp/p_followups/loft_loopvar_type_conflict.loft` as the documented-limitation reference.  **Status 2026-05-26:** the skill BODY already states the accurate rule (`SKILL.md:553-560`, "reuse is allowed as long as the type stays consistent"); only the pre-flight checklist line (`SKILL.md:713`, "All loop variables are unique across the entire file") still over-states it.  The one-line checklist correction was attempted but BLOCKED by the harness auto-mode classifier (editing an agent-loaded skill file is self-modification needing explicit user authorization) — so the corrected checklist line needs the user to allow that edit, or it can be done by hand: change it to *"Reused loop-var names keep a consistent type (distinct names only needed when element types differ — @P344)."*  Option (B) deliberately NOT implemented (core-model change, Low severity, trivial workaround).  **Resolved 2026-06-01 via option (A):** documented as an accepted-as-intended limitation in [CAVEATS.md § P344](CAVEATS.md) + a Verification-log row; the diagnostic is already regression-guarded (`tests/parse_errors.rs::shadow_different_type`, `tests/scripts/36-parse-errors.loft:185`), so no new test was needed.  ONE residual, flagged for the user: the loft-write skill pre-flight checklist line (`SKILL.md:819`, "All loop variables are unique across the entire file") still over-states the rule — correcting it needs explicit authorization to edit the agent-loaded skill (the skill BODY at 553-560 is already accurate).  Option (B) (true per-loop scoping) remains a deferred core-resolver/slot-model change. | 🟢 RESOLVED 2026-06-01 (intended; CAVEATS.md § P344) | Use distinct loop-variable names when the element types differ (`for n in …` / `for s in …`). |
| 343 | **@P343** — **FIXED 2026-05-26 (both backends)** — Iterating a `vector<fn(...)>` of NAMED fn-refs with a `for` loop mis-dispatches: `fs: vector<fn(integer)->integer> = [dbl]; for f in fs { total += apply(f); }` returns **0** on the interpreter (expected `20`) and fails `--native` compile (E0600).  Index access works — `f = fs[0]; apply(f)` returns `20` on both backends (that path was fixed by @P214) — only the for-loop iteration path was missed, so a loop variable bound from a fn-ref vector element doesn't carry the callable.  Previously tracked ONLY as the ignored test `tests/issues.rs::p4d_a2_vector_fn_ref_for_loop` with a STALE ignore reason (`"parse fails 'Expect token ;'"` — the parse bug is gone; this is now a runtime/codegen wrong-result).  Its sibling `p4d_a2_vector_fn_ref_index_access` now PASSES and should be un-ignored.  Repro: `/tmp/p_followups/loft_vec_fnref_forloop.loft` (interp → `0`; `--native` → E0600).  Likely fix site: the for-loop element-binding codegen for `Type::Function` vector content (`src/parser/collections.rs` for-loop materialisation + `src/generation` call-of-loop-var), mirroring the @P214 index-apply fix into the iteration path.  **Diagnosis (2026-05-26, partial — reverted unshipped):** the for-loop's fn-ref read in `parse_for_iter_setup` (`src/parser/collections.rs:~237`) diverges from the WORKING `fs[i]` index-apply path (`parser/fields.rs:730` @P214) in TWO ways — (1) element STRIDE: the for-loop computes `size = database.size(db_tp)` = **0** for the function content type (should be 4, the d_nr stride; index-apply uses `OpGetVector(fs, 4, i)`); (2) read SHAPE: the for-loop routes through `get_val(Type::Function, …)` whose arm reads a struct `__closure_rec` child via `OpRefFromChildRec(OpGetField(elem, 4, 0))` — but vector elements have NO `__closure_rec`, so it reads garbage; the index-apply path instead pairs the d_nr with `OpNullRefSentinel()` (non-capturing, no closure).  Fixing BOTH (size→4 + a vector-aware `OpGetInt4`+`OpNullRefSentinel` block in the `Type::Function` branch) brings the IR to byte-parity with index-apply, BUT the loop STILL returns 0 — a THIRD layer remains (the `OpGetVectorNullable`-based read still yields a null/`OpNot`-true loop var where `OpGetVector` does not, or the loop-var call path differs), and `--native` still fails E0600 separately.  Needs a dedicated session (P214-class, multi-part).  **Fix design (2026-05-26) — THIRD LAYER root-caused:** the third layer is the loop's TERMINATION test, not the element read.  At `src/parser/collections.rs:1697-1704` a non-coroutine vector loop builds `test_for = OpNot(convert(Var(for_var), var_tp, Boolean))`.  For `var_tp = Type::Function`, the fn-ref is a 2-part stack value `(d_nr: u32, closure: DbRef)`; `convert(Function, Boolean)` tests the closure half (which the vector read ALWAYS sets to `OpNullRefSentinel`, since vector fn-ref elements are non-capturing) → the test is null on EVERY iteration → `OpNot` → true → `break` fires on iteration 0 → loop body never runs → `total` stays `0`.  This is the SAME hazard the code already special-cases for *coroutine* fn-ref yields at `1682-1696` (the `yield_needs_exhausted_check` branch added by @PLAN16 phase 05, terminating via `OpCoroutineExhausted` instead of the yielded value) — but a *vector* fn-ref loop has no coroutine to ask, so it wrongly falls into the value-test `else if` at 1697.  **Three coordinated changes for the interpreter half:** (1) element STRIDE — in the `iterator()` `Type::Vector` arm (`collections.rs:237-245`), add a `matches!(**vtp, Type::Function(..)) => 4` case so the `OpGetVectorNullable` stride is the 4-byte d_nr (today `database.size(db_tp)` = 0); (2) element READ — in the `else` at 269-279, special-case `Type::Function` to emit the explicit `OpGetInt4(elem,0)` + `OpNullRefSentinel` `"fn_ref_field_read"` block exactly like the working index path (`parser/fields.rs:730-752`), NOT `get_val(Function)` (which reads a non-existent `__closure_rec`); (3) TERMINATION — drive the break off the underlying ELEMENT DbRef's nullity (the `OpGetVectorNullable` result, which IS null at OOB) rather than `convert(fnref,Boolean)` — simplest: keep the raw element DbRef in a hidden var and test THAT, or read the d_nr and break when the element slot is OOB (`index >= len`).  **Native half (E0600, separate):** the loop-var-call codegen in `src/generation/` must emit the fn-ref call the same way the index-apply path does post-@P214 (call through `(d_nr as usize, closure)`); E0600 = "cannot find function/loop-var-as-callable" means the native emitter doesn't recognise the loop-bound fn-ref as callable — mirror the index-apply emit.  **Test:** un-ignore `tests/issues.rs::p4d_a2_vector_fn_ref_for_loop` (interp → 20) and `p4d_a2_vector_fn_ref_index_access` (already passes), add a `cross_mode!` so native is gated too.  Still multi-part (interp 3-change + native emit), but all three interp layers are now precisely located.  **FIXED 2026-05-26 (all three layers + native, one coordinated patch):** `src/parser/collections.rs` `iterator()` `Type::Vector` arm now (1) uses stride **4** for `Type::Function` content (was `database.size`=0), and (2) reads the element as a `fn_ref_field_read` block `OpGetInt4(elem,0)` + `OpNullRefSentinel` (mirrors the @P214 index path; was the struct-field `__closure_rec` read).  (3) The for-loop termination gained a `Type::Vector` + `Type::Function` branch that breaks on `OpNot(OpLtInt(0, FnRefDnr(for_var)))` — i.e. break once the loop's d_nr is no longer a valid (callable, `> 0`) function ref.  This is the single test that works on BOTH backends: at out-of-bounds the d_nr is i64::MIN on the interpreter (`OpGetInt4` on the `OpGetVectorNullable` null sentinel) and `0u32` on `--native` (that i64::MIN truncates in the `(u32, DbRef)` fn-ref tuple, which is also native's invalid-fn marker) — both `<= 0`, while every real fn d_nr is `> 0`.  `FnRefDnr` already projects the d_nr on both backends (interp `OpVarInt`, native `var_f.0`), so the native loop-var call (`n_apply(cell, var_f)`) just worked once termination stopped the loop reaching the OOB element.  Regression: `tests/scripts/repro_p343.loft` (single + multi-fn + index-parity, runs under interp via `wrap.rs` and native via `native.rs::native_scripts`); `tests/issues.rs::{p4d_a2_vector_fn_ref_for_loop,p4d_a2_vector_fn_ref_index_access}` un-ignored. | ~~Low~~ FIXED | n/a (fixed) — was: index instead of iterate: `i = 0; while i < len(fs) { total += apply(fs[i]); i += 1 }`. |

| 339 | **@P339** — **FIXED 2026-05-26** — `lib/graphics` text rendering applied NO kerning.  `gl_measure_text("AV", 40)` returned `61.914…` == `measure("A")+measure("V")` exactly (A–V is a textbook negative-kern pair).  `measure_text` summed only `metrics.advance_width`; `rasterize_text` advanced `x_cursor += advance_width` — neither consulted the kern table.  **Fix** (`lib/graphics/native/src/text.rs`): both now track the previous char and add fontdue's `font.horizontal_kern(prev, c, size)` — `measure_text` to the width sum, `rasterize_text` via a float pen (`x_pen += kern` before each glyph) so measurement and layout agree.  Verified: `gl_measure_text("AV", 40)` = `59.20` < `A+V` = `61.91`.  Regression `lib/graphics/tests/kerning.loft` (both backends). | 338 | **@P338** — **FIXED 2026-05-26** — `--native` codegen E0499 when a vector is indexed with an index expression that itself uses `&mut stores` — specifically a **checked division/modulo in the index**: `v[n / 2]` emits Rust where the checked-div's `raise_runtime` (div-by-zero) borrow AND `vec_get_or_raise_runtime`'s borrow are both live in the same expression → *"cannot borrow `*stores` as mutable more than once at a time"*.  Interpreter is fine; only `--native` (the default) breaks.  Same family as **@P321d** (nested-index double-borrow, fixed by binding `@r` to a local in the `OpGetVector`/`OpVectorRef` `#rust` templates in `default/01_code.loft`) — but @P321d hoisted the receiver, not a `&mut`-using INDEX subexpression.  Fix: the index argument to `vec_get_or_raise`/`OpGetVector` must also be bound to a local before the outer `&mut stores` call (e.g. `{{let __vi = <index>; let __vr = @r; s.vec_get_or_raise(&__vr, __vi, …)}}`), so the div's borrow ends before the vec-get borrow starts.  Repro: `/tmp/p_followups/loft_index_div_borrow.loft` (native → E0499; `--interpret` → `2`).  Workaround (no longer needed): hoist the index — `mid = n / 2; v[mid]`.  **FIXED 2026-05-26**: the `OpGetVector` / `OpVectorRef` `#rust` templates (`default/01_code.loft`) now bind `@index` to a local (`let __vi = @index;`) after `@r`, before the `vec_get_or_raise` / `vec_ref_or_raise` call — so the index expression's borrow ends before the receiver's `&mut self` borrow starts.  `make fill` regenerated; both backends print `2` for `v[3/2]`.  Regression: `tests/scripts/repro_p338.loft` (div / mod / nested-read indices, both backends). | ~~Low~~ FIXED | n/a (fixed) — was: hoist the index into a local before the `[ ]`. | 
| 337 | **@P337** — **CORRECTED + FIXED 2026-05-26** — "Brick Buster broken on the site / page times out."  **The original diagnosis (a `vector<float>` length read back too-short on wasm32, `panic_bounds_check` in `build_mvp_2d`) is DISPROVEN.**  Verification: a minimal repro (16-elem `vector<float>` literal in a struct field, index `[15]`) AND a faithful copy of `build_mvp_2d` (computed-expression projection passed as `const vector<float>`, indexing `proj[0..15]` + building a new 16-float vector) BOTH read back `len==16` and `[15]` correctly on the wasm32-unknown-unknown `--html` browser build — identical to interpreter + `--native` — even after `wasm-opt -O1 --asyncify`. Two independent ways the shipped bundle breaks, both passing the old size/DOCTYPE sanity check: **(a) rlib STOMP** — after `make wasm`, the rlib carries `feature=wasm` → `wasm-bindgen`/`js_sys`, so `loft --html` emits a wasm importing `__wbindgen_placeholder__` (35+) that the embedded `doc/loft-gl-wasm.js` glue (raw `loft_gl`/`loft_io` externs only) does not provide → the page fails to instantiate (`Import … __wbindgen_placeholder__: module is not an object`).  **(b) MISSING wasm-opt** — without binaryen the `--asyncify` pass never runs, so there is no frame-yield; the HTML driver calls `loft_start()` synchronously and brick-buster's `for _ in 0..10000000` render loop blocks the browser main thread forever → "page times out."  The working-tree `doc/brick-buster.html` had been rebuilt into state (b) (correct rlib, no asyncify) in a `wasm-opt`-less session; a fresh `loft --html` after the recent doc/pkg `make wasm` reproduces state (a).  **Reproduction method (now the canonical one):** `cargo build --release --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --lib --no-default-features --features random` (rebuild rlib in --html shape) → `loft --html out.html prog.loft` → extract `wasmB64` → instantiate under Node (`tools/check_html_bundle.mjs` / `tools/wasm_repro.mjs`) and/or render in headless Chromium via `puppeteer-core`.  **FIXED 2026-05-26 (toolchain + hardening, per the chosen scope):** (1) `tools/check_html_bundle.mjs` — static import/export gate that fails on wbindgen imports (stomp) or a missing `asyncify_start_unwind` export (no frame-yield), wired into `make game` step 6 so a broken bundle never ships; (2) `loft --html` now warns LOUDLY when `wasm-opt` is absent (the bundle will hang, not merely be larger); (3) `make doctor` reports the full wasm/native toolchain status; (4) the rlib-stomp ordering rule + toolchain table documented in [WASM.md § Build Toolchain Dependencies](WASM.md#build-toolchain-dependencies).  **Remaining manual step (not auto-applied):** the uncommitted working-tree `doc/brick-buster.html` is still the broken (no-asyncify) rebuild — regenerate it with `make game` (now has the integrity gate) or `git checkout doc/brick-buster.html` to restore the working committed bundle. | ~~High~~ FIXED (was a misdiagnosed runtime bug; real cause is the build pipeline, now gated) | Build via `make game` (rebuilds the rlib in --html shape + runs the integrity gate), not a bare `loft --html` after `make wasm`.  Ensure `wasm-opt` (binaryen) is installed — see `make doctor`. |

The resize handler (`lib/graphics/native/src/lib.rs`, `PENDING_RESIZE`) called `gl::Viewport(0,0,w,h)` but never updated `s.viewport_w`/`s.viewport_h` — the values the getters return. | 335 | **@P335** — **FIXED 2026-05-25** — A user identifier spelled `n_<X>` collided with a function named `<X>` in scope, because user functions are stored under the mangled name `n_<X>` and the parser resolved a RAW identifier against the def table.  So a variable `n_day` aliases function `day` (stored `n_day`): `n_day: vector<text> = []` → *"Expect token ;"* at the `:`; `n_day = […]` → *"Cannot redefine function 'n_day' as a variable"*.  **Trigger in the wild:** `use time;` (or any non-glob use of a lib that exports short names) — `lib/time` exports `day`/`year`/`month`/`hour`/`minute`/`second`, so a plain `use time;` makes variables named `n_day`/`n_year`/… fail to parse even though nothing calls `time::`.  NOT import-specific: a local `fn day() {}` + a variable `n_day` reproduces it with zero imports; renaming the fn (or the var) fixes it.  Repro: `/tmp/p_followups/loft_use_time_bug.loft` (and the 4-line `fn day()` + `n_day` minimal case).  **Site:** `src/parser/objects.rs:236` (`def_nr(name)` raw lookup, no `:`-decl guard — only `=` is guarded) and the `:267` fallback `if nr==MAX { def_nr(&nm) }`.  **Fix:** resolve a user identifier to a function ONLY via the `n_`+ident mangle (`def_nr("n_"+nm)`); the raw-name fallback (kept for enums/types stored un-prefixed) must REJECT `DefType::Function` matches — so `n_day` looks up `n_n_day` (miss) and binds as a variable.  Variables need no mangle; the leak is that user idents can spell the `n_`-mangled function namespace.  Root alternative: a function-mangle prefix illegal in identifiers (heavier — `n_<name>` is pervasive).  **DONE:** both raw-lookup sites in `src/parser/objects.rs` now exclude `DefType::Function` — the raw `def_nr(name)` enum/type branch gains `&& !matches!(def_type(...), Function)`, and the function-ref fallback only accepts a raw match when it is NOT a Function — so functions are reachable only via the `n_`+ident mangle (`n_day` → `n_n_day`, miss → binds as a variable).  Normal code is unaffected: a non-`n_` identifier's raw lookup never matched a function (all functions are `n_`-prefixed).  Pinned by `tests/scripts/repro_p335.loft` (typed decl + assignment + the colliding functions still callable; interp + native). | ~~Medium~~ FIXED | n/a (fixed) — was: rename the variable away from the `n_` prefix. |

(b) **hash/index** collection ops — a `room`/bucket computation or `claim` size that overflows 32-bit `usize` on wasm32 (same width hazard flagged for @P337) and trips a debug bounds-check.  **Fix shape:** once localized, add the missing wasm-side guard / preopen handling (file path) or width-correct the collection arithmetic; pin by un-skipping `world` from `LIB_PKGS_WASM_SKIP` so `tests/html_wasm.rs::wasm_library_suite` covers it. That alone makes any file-using lib trap.  Fix the harness to pass `--dir <tmp>` (and route the lib's save/load path under that dir).  **(2) Re-narrowed 2026-05-29 — the `world_load` bug was actually NOT in the parser:** the 2026-05-26 "width/sign read" hypothesis was misleading. Manual `loft install <pkg>` against the real registry on Windows has not been validated end-to-end; this gate is a "tests-only" green pass, not a Windows-supported claim.  **FIXED 2026-05-26 — root-caused:** the install/extract path resolves its home via `registry_index::cache_dir()` → `dirs::home_dir()`, which reads `$HOME` on Unix but `USERPROFILE`/the FOLDERID_Profile known folder on Windows — it does NOT honour `$HOME` there.  The e2e test isolates the registry by setting `HOME=<tmpdir>`; on Windows that override was IGNORED, so installs leaked into the real user profile and cross-run caching made `install_one` see a stale `loft.toml` → every package routed to `skipped_cached` → `installed.len()==0`.  Fix: `cache_dir()` now reads a new `LOFT_HOME` env var FIRST (cross-platform, identical everywhere), falling back to `dirs::home_dir()`; `HomeGuard` (`tests/registry_e2e.rs`) sets `LOFT_HOME` alongside `HOME`.  Removed both `#[cfg(not(windows))]` gates; all 5 `registry_e2e` tests pass on Linux (the two formerly-gated ones included).  Production behaviour unchanged (no `LOFT_HOME` set → `dirs::home_dir()`).  Could not run a Windows runner directly, but the fix removes the platform-divergent construct (`$HOME` reliance) — `LOFT_HOME` is honoured identically on Windows.  **Follow-up (consistency, not blocking):** `src/registry.rs` (legacy `registry.txt`/`lib` paths) and the `extensions.rs` cdylib loader still resolve `~/.loft` via their own `HOME`/`USERPROFILE` logic; routing ALL `~/.loft` resolution through one `LOFT_HOME`-aware helper would also let registry-cdylib tests (cf. @P331) isolate cross-platform. | ~~Low~~ FIXED | n/a (fixed) — was: use macOS or Linux for registry workflows until Windows hardening lands. |
| 331 | **@P331** — **FIXED 2026-05-27 (superseded by @P370 / @PLAN48 P1, commit `42af45d1`)** — Registry-installed cdylib `n_rand(lo, hi)` returning `i64::MIN` (loft's integer null sentinel) is observed as `0` by the caller in a `loft --interpret` run loaded via `use random;` from `~/.loft/registry/random-0.1.0/`.  Repro: `cd /tmp/randscratch && loft install random && cat > x.loft <<EOF\nuse random;\nfn main() { v = rand(10, 5); print("v={v}\n"); }\nEOF\nloft --interpret x.loft` → prints `v=0` (expected `v=` + null rendering).  The cdylib source (`~/.loft/registry/random-0.1.0/native/src/lib.rs`) explicitly returns `i64::MIN` on `lo > hi`; the same logic AS BUILT BY THE PACKAGE TEST RUNNER (`cd /Users/Jurjen/Documents/loft-libs-core/random && loft --interpret test`) yields the correct null (test_rand_invalid_range passes).  Difference: registry-installed `use random;` vs in-package `loft test` dispatch — two distinct cdylib-loading paths.  Looks like i64→i32 truncation somewhere on the registry-load path (i64::MIN as i32 = 0).  **Discovered** while greening chunk CI (plan-12 phase 6.5); not blocking CI (the package test runner is what CI uses).  Workaround: use `loft test` from the package dir, or compare against an explicit i64::MIN check rather than `!rand(...)`.  **Fix design (2026-05-26) — root cause located:** the truncation is in the native-extension dispatcher's ABI classification.  `src/extensions.rs:218-220` (and `:238-245`) maps `Type::Integer(s) if s.is_wide() => ArgT::I64` but `Type::Integer(_) | Type::Character => ArgT::I32` — so a NARROW `integer` return (which `rand(lo,hi)->integer` is) selects the `(&[I32,I32], Some(I32)) => extern "C" fn(i32,i32)->i32` dispatch arm at `:792-794`.  The cdylib's `n_rand` returns `i64::MIN` on `lo>hi`; calling it through an `-> i32` fn-pointer makes the C ABI hand back only the low 32 bits = `0x00000000` = `0`, and `widen_int(0)` (`:717-721`, which only rescues `i32::MIN`) leaves it `0`.  The `loft test` path agrees because that flow dispatches the SAME cdylib consistently; the registry-`use` path diverges by classifying the return as I32.  **Why not a one-line fix:** simply reclassifying `Type::Integer(_) => ArgT::I64` is forward-correct (registry cdylibs are SOURCE-BUILT on `loft install` by the CURRENT native emitter, which emits `-> i64` for `-> integer` post-2c — so there are no stale pre-built i32 cdylibs to break) BUT it forces EVERY integer-typed native arg/return across ALL packages (`imaging`, `graphics`, `world`, …) onto the I64 ABI, which needs new `(&[I64,I64], Some(I64))`-shaped dispatch arms in the big `match` at `:757+` (most don't exist yet) AND must be co-verified against `native_library_suite` (53 cdylibs).  That is an M-effort ABI-version alignment (bump `loft_register_v1` semantics so emitter + dispatcher share ONE integer-width rule), not a safe immediate patch — and it cannot be validated on a box without a registry round-trip + cdylib rebuild.  **Recommended:** schedule as a small dedicated arc: (1) audit emitter output width for `integer` cdylib exports, (2) flip dispatcher classification to match, (3) add the missing I64 dispatch arms, (4) re-green `native_library_suite`, (5) add a `random`-style `rand(hi<lo)->null` regression to the registry e2e suite.  Deferred rather than blind-patched to avoid destabilising the 53-cdylib native gate.  **FIXED 2026-05-27 — superseded by @P370 / @PLAN48 P1 (commit `42af45d1`, PR #221), which executed exactly this arc:** the dispatcher classification was flipped to `Type::Integer(_) if forced_size.is_none() => ArgT::I64` (`src/extensions.rs:282-283`) and the full family of I64 dispatch arms added — the `(&[I64,I64], Some(I64)) => extern "C" fn(i64,i64)->i64` arm at `:1602` reads the i64 return untruncated, so `i64::MIN` survives.  The "two distinct dispatch paths" framing was a misdiagnosis: both `use random;` (`main.rs:4185`) and in-package `loft test` (`main.rs:5341`) funnel through the one shared `dispatch_call`; the real fault was purely the return-type classification, now correct.  Regression: random's `test_rand_invalid_range` (asserts `rand(hi<lo) → null`) runs in `native_library_suite`.  (Optional step-5 follow-up: a registry-install e2e variant — but the shared dispatch makes recurrence impossible.) | 🟢 FIXED 2026-05-27 (superseded by @P370) | n/a — fixed. |
| 330 | **@P330** — **FIXED 2026-05-24** — Pre-existing parser bug surfaced while writing @P329 regression coverage.  A generic fn returning `(text, text)` whose result was assigned to a local and then element-accessed for the caller's text return — `fn take_first<T: Printable>(p: T) -> text { r = pair(p); r.0 }` — crashed the interpreter (SIGBUS at op=128 in the callee's frame) and emitted broken native (`return (&var_x.to_string(), …)` = `&String` E0308).  **Root cause:** `parser/control.rs::text_return` (the auto-hoist that promotes locals whose deps reach the returned text into hidden RefVar/Text parameters) had a non-text fallback at lines 2950-2956 that hoisted the local with its ORIGINAL type — so a `Type::Tuple([text, text])` local became a `(text, text)` parameter.  But the call site can only push a 12-byte null `DbRef` placeholder for the hoisted arg (`InitRefSentinel`), while the parameter slot is 32 bytes for `(text, text)` (16 per `Str` element).  The 20-byte size mismatch corrupted the callee's frame layout, so every subsequent argument read returned a garbage `ref(7, 0, …)`.  Native side: my new @P329 `Value::Var(__ret_text_1)` in a `Return(Tuple(…))` position emitted `&var___ret_text_1` (the standard `String → &str` coerce at `src/generation/emit.rs:153`), then the surrounding `tuple_text_to_string` wrap added `.to_string()` — but Rust method-call precedence parses `&var.to_string()` as `&(var.to_string())` = `&String`, which the declared `String` tuple slot rejects.  **Fix (two coordinated changes):**  (1) `parser/control.rs::text_return`: when the local being hoisted is a `Type::Tuple`, `continue` past the hoist — do NOT add the attribute, do NOT promote, do NOT propagate the dep to the return type.  The function's return type loses the tuple-local dep, so `scopes::free_vars`'s B5-L3 single-text branch (`src/scopes.rs:961-988`) saves the body's tail `r.0` to a `__ret_N: text` via `Set` (deep-copy through `OpAppendText`) before the tuple local is freed.  (2) `src/generation/emit.rs::Value::Var` for text locals: when `tuple_text_to_string` is true, emit bare `var_x` instead of `&var_x` so the wrap produces `var_x.to_string()` (owned `String` clone) — `String`'s `to_string()` clones, satisfying the tuple slot's declared `String` type.  Pinned by `tests/issues.rs::p330_generic_tuple_return_assign_then_chain_first` + `p330_generic_tuple_return_assign_then_chain_second` (chained `.0` and `.1` shapes; both backends green). | ~~Medium~~ FIXED | n/a (fixed) — historical workaround was to chain the tuple-elem access directly on the call result (`pair().0`, routes through `parser/operators.rs:597-615` temp-tuple branch which @P329 already fixed). |

Generalisation of the B5-L3 single-text pattern at lines 961-988.  Pinned by un-ignored `tests/issues.rs::p243_bounded_generic_tuple_with_text_method_call` plus 3 new `p329_*` regressions covering `(text, integer)`, `(text, text, text)`, and second-element access shapes. | 316 | **@P316** — **FIXED 2026-05-22.**  Native miscompile: reading a NARROW-int vector element (`vector<u8>`, and likely `vector<i16>`/`vector<i32>`) with the null-coalesce default `coll[i] ?? <int-literal>` generated Rust `(if … {u8_elem} else {0_i64}) as u8` → `error[E0308]: mismatched types` (the `??` result was typed as the narrow element type `u8`, so native couldn't unify the `if` branches — they kept their own native widths because `convert(Integer→Integer)` leniently returns `true` WITHOUT actually re-typing). The branches then share a native type and the result matches the surrounding integer arithmetic; widening is always safe (only narrowing is disallowed, @P315).  Matching-width integers and non-integer types keep the original behaviour (bring the default to the value's type). | 318 | **@P318** — **FIXED 2026-05-22.**  Deep-copying a STRUCT whose `hash<T[k]>` field is populated corrupted the hash's ENTRIES on the copy — size-dependent + NON-deterministic.  **ROOT (proven by unit test):** a hash reads its `room` (bucket count, `elms=(room-1)*2`) from the record's SIZE HEADER (offset 0), and `Store::claim` returns a block up to 1/3 larger than requested WITHOUT splitting (`claim_block`: no split when `block_size <= req*4/3`).  `copy_claims_hash_body`'s slot-for-slot copy laid the dest buckets out for the SOURCE's room, but the dest record's header (its own `room`) could differ, so `hash::find` later probed `key % dest_elms` — a DIFFERENT start slot — and missed entries.  The over-size gap `(room, room*4/3]` is TINY for small rooms ((9,12], (17,22]) so a freed record easily lands in it; for the crystal `cidx` (room 513) the gap (514,684] is rarely hit, which is why it surfaced only intermittently as @P317 symptom 1 (sidestepped there by building the field in place — that remains, and is also faster).  Earlier "build_index/OpReplaceKeyed-specific / layout-dependent" framing was the pre-root-cause hypothesis; the real trigger is the dest over-size, reachable for ANY populated-hash deep copy.  **FIX:** `copy_claims_hash_body` now re-INSERTS each entry into an emptied destination via `hash::add` (rebuilding the dest consistent with its OWN room — exactly as the source was built), mirroring `copy_claims_index_body`; this also subsumes the earlier @P317 length-word and @P290 loop-bound fixes.  Regression: `src/database/allocation.rs` unit test `p318_hash_deepcopy::hash_deepcopy_survives_oversized_dest_bucket` forces the over-size deterministically (claim `big / gap=room+1 / big`, delete the middle so `fl_take_ge(room)` returns the gap) — loses 14/14 entries before the fix, 0 after.  Validated: full suite green. | ~~Medium~~ FIXED | n/a (fixed). |
| 315 | **@P315** — **FIXED 2026-05-22.**  A `vector<single>` built from FLOAT LITERALS wrote each element **8 bytes wide (`OpSetFloat`/f64)** into a backing whose stride is **4 bytes** (`single`, `size=4`), overflowing the slot.  **FIX:** `parse_item` (`src/parser/vectors.rs`) no longer silently PROMOTES a DECLARED element type (e.g. `Single`→`Float`); when the declared element type is non-`Unknown` and a value does not convert TO it, it now ERRORS ("cannot store float elements in a vector<single> (would lose precision); cast each element explicitly with 'as single'") — consistent with scalar / local-vector assignment, and honouring the user rule "no silent truncation of float→single".  `[1.0f, 2.0f]` (single literals, `f` suffix), `[1 as single, …]`, or single-typed variables work; `[1, 2]` (int) still works (`int→single`); inferred untyped literals still promote.  Threaded a `declared` flag `parse_vector`→`collect_vector_items`→`parse_item`.  Regression: `tests/scripts/134-vector-single-width.loft`.  Validated: wrap/issues/native/leak/threading/doc_hygiene green.  HISTORICAL root detail: a `vector<single>` built from FLOAT LITERALS wrote each element 8 bytes wide.  Minimal 12-line, hash-free, deterministic SIGSEGV repro (`/tmp/p_followups/p315_min_nested_vector_single.loft`): `struct Sm { a: vector<single> } struct Ent { k: integer not null, m: Sm } fn main() { e = Ent{k:1, m: Sm{a:[1.0,2.0]}}; println("{len(e.m.a)}"); }`.  **Narrowing (all verified):** `vector<integer>` (8-byte, matches `OpSetInt`) → works; binding the inner struct to a local → works; single-level `vector<single>` → works (the over-write lands in the vector's slack and survives without relocation); a struct-returning literal that is **RELOCATED** via `OpCopyRecord` (struct-field assign, keyed-collection insert, `seg_mesh_append`) is where the over-written backing becomes fatal.  **Mechanism (valgrind + IR):** the element value `1.0` is a `single` (the IR even wraps it `OpConvFloatFromSingle`), but the element-write op resolves to `OpSetFloat` because the vector-element set goes through `set_field(def, f_nr = usize::MAX, …)` (`src/parser/mod.rs:2947`), and `attr_type(single_def, usize::MAX)` returns the wide `Float` "value" type → the `Type::Float => OpSetFloat` arm (mod.rs:3158) instead of `Type::Single => OpSetSingle` (3159).  `vector_append` (`structures.rs:41`) and `copy_claims_seq_vector` (`allocation.rs:799`) both correctly stride by `size(single)=4`, so the WRITE (8-byte) is the sole inconsistency.  (Initially suspected as @P314's root — DISPROVEN: @P314's actual root was a separate NARROW-VECTOR CONCAT bug on the `u8` `kinds`/`colors` fields, not the `single` coords; see @P314.)  SegMesh's own build survives because it appends single VARIABLES (`s.x0s += [single_var]`, element type `Single` → `OpSetSingle`) not float literals.  **Fix direction:** make `vector<single>` element writes use `OpSetSingle` (4-byte) — either coerce float literals to `single` against the target vector content type, or special-case `single` content in the vector-element `set_field`/`new_record`/comprehension paths (mirror the narrow-integer arm at `vectors.rs:1921`).  Central to `single`-vector handling (SegMesh, all graphics) → needs careful regression validation (130 / segmesh.loft / leak).  Earlier "keyed-iteration / copy_claims recursion" hypothesis DISPROVEN (no hash needed). | High (heap corruption / SIGSEGV / silent data corruption) | Use `vector<integer>` where possible; or build `vector<single>` by appending single-typed VARIABLES (`v += [x]` with `x: single`) rather than float literals; or avoid relocating (deep-copying into a struct field / keyed collection) a `vector<single>` built from literals. |
| 310 | **@P310** — **Closed (fixed) 2026-05-21** — Native codegen passed a **`*const i32`** data pointer for a `const vector<integer>` argument bound to a `vec<i64>` FFI wrapper → `error[E0308]: expected *const i64, found *const i32`, breaking every graphics FFI taking a 64-bit-element vector (`loft_save_png`, `loft_gl_upload_canvas`, `loft_rasterize_text_into`) under `--native`/`--check`. **Root cause:** `src/generation/mod.rs::vector_elem_rust_type()` chose the FFI pointer element type from `IntegerSpec::is_wide()` — a VALUE-RANGE predicate (`max > i32::MAX`). A plain `integer` post-2c is the signed-32 template (`is_wide() == false`) yet its vector storage stride is 8 bytes (`forced_size: None` → `byte_width() == 8`), so codegen emitted a 4-byte `*const i32` for 8-byte-stride data, contradicting both the storage and the `vec<i64>` wrapper. (Pre-2c the storage really was i32 — the comment was stale.) **Fix (2026-05-21):** key `vector_elem_rust_type` off the STORAGE stride via `IntegerSpec::vector_narrow_width()` (the same predicate the vector storage + `OpGetVector`/`OpSetVector` use): `None` → `i64` (plain `vector<integer>`), `Some(4/2/1)` → `i32`/`i16`/`u8` (narrow aliases keep their forced width). One match arm; the graphics externs already declared `*const i64` so they now match, and no cdylib extern takes `*const i32` for a vector arg. Guards: unit test `generation::p310_vector_elem_tests` (pins plain integer → `i64`) + end-to-end `tests/codegen_emitter.rs::p310_graphics_vector_ffi_checks_clean` (`--check`s a canvas+`save_png` fixture). Both backends; canvas+save_png+gl_upload_canvas now `--check` clean. (crystal_render now hits the unrelated @P312 E0503 next.) | Medium (closed) | Fixed — was: run graphics consumers under `--interpret`. |

| 305 | **@P305** — **Closed 2026-05-21** — Indexed-assignment into a keyed collection, `h[key] = value`, **updated an existing key in place but silently NO-OPed when the key was absent** — it never inserted. Sibling of the `h[key] = null` removal path, which DOES work (`towards_set_hash_remove`, `src/parser/collections.rs:443`).  **Fixed 2026-05-21** with a new op `OpSetKeyed(coll, value, tp)` (find-or-insert, deduped by `value`'s key) emitted for keyed `coll[key] = value`.  The old lowering `OpCopyRecord(value, OpGetRecord(coll, key))` copied into the lookup slot — fine for an existing key, but `OpGetRecord` returned null on a miss so the copy no-op'd (no insert).  `Stores::set_keyed` (`src/database/search.rs`) finds `value`'s key; if present it frees + unlinks the old record (and reclaims the orphaned hash claim so repeated replaces don't grow the store), then deep-copies `value` into a fresh record and links it via the proven `record_new`/`copy_claims`/`record_finish` path — uniformly for local / struct-field / `&`-param collections (sorted/index too).  Pieces: bodyless op decl in `default/01_code.loft` (interp `s.set_keyed()`); `State::set_keyed`; native `codegen_runtime::OpSetKeyed` + dispatch arm; parser intercept in `towards_set` (`src/parser/collections.rs`); a `generate_var` arm so a `&`-keyed param can be referenced (`OpGetStackRef`); `OpSetKeyed` added to the `first_arg_write` lists.  **Multi-index guard**: a struct with two-plus keyed fields sharing an element type auto-links them (`other_indexes`); `coll[key]=value` on such a field is rejected with a diagnostic (use `+= [value]`, which maintains the siblings) since `OpSetKeyed` can't update them.  Both backends; bounded memory under replace churn; gridmesh's `f.dirty[ck] = ChunkKey{…}` dedup idiom works. | 306 | **@P306** — **Closed (fixed) 2026-05-21** — `keyed += [entry]` did **not dedup** on key collision: inserting an entry whose key already existed appended a SECOND entry rather than replacing (`len` grew; `h[key]` returned the FIRST, the later shadowed but still occupying space).  Both backends; `hash`/`sorted`/`index` alike.  **Confirmed** (`/tmp/p_followups/p306_keyed_append_no_dedup.loft`): `h += [Ent{ck:10,v:1}]; h += [Ent{ck:10,v:9}]` → `len=2`, `h[10].v == 1`.  A keyed collection must treat the key as identity — re-inserting a key should REPLACE (latest wins).  Surfaced 2026-05-21 (`gridmesh` dirty index + the world-chunk index `hash<Chunk[cx,cy,cz]>`, where inserting a chunk at an occupied coord must replace, not stack a shadowed dup — a correctness requirement, lib-plan 19).  Initially closed-by-decision (C68: keep `+=` append, use `coll[key]=value` for dedup) but **reversed + implemented the same day** once the chunk-index requirement made dedup-on-insert correctness, not preference.  **Fix**: dedup in the shared insert path so BOTH `+=` and `coll[key]=value` dedup.  Hash + index (separate-claim records): new `Stores::dedup_keyed` (`src/database/search.rs`) — find existing by the new record's key, free its nested heap, unlink it, reclaim its slot — called from `insert_record` (`src/database/structures.rs`) before `hash::add` / `tree::add` (the latter otherwise rejected the dup and kept the OLD).  Sorted: `vector::sorted_finish` uses `sorted_find`'s `found` flag to overwrite the existing slot in place instead of growing.  Both backends share `record_finish`→`insert_record`, so one fix covers interp + native.  Full suite green — no consumer relied on duplicate-key append.  Guards: `tests/scripts/127-keyed-indexed-set.loft` (`+=` dedup for hash/sorted/index, latest-wins, sorted order preserved) + hash_spec C09/C10.  See [C68](DESIGN_DECISIONS_COLLECTIONS.md#c68--keyed-collections-dedup-on-insert--and-collkeyvalue) for the reverse rationale.  Spec + matrix: [plans/finished/44-hash-semantics/](plans/finished/44-hash-semantics/README.md). | Medium (closed) | Fixed — no workaround needed. |

Sibling of @P295 (keyed-local reassignment) / @P296 (keyed-local native).  **Confirmed** (2026-05-21, `/tmp/p_followups/p300_hash_return_assign.loft`): `fn mk() -> hash<R[ck]> { h: hash<R[ck]> = []; h += [R{ck:5, ix:9}]; h }  fn main() { x = mk(); … }` panics; passing a pre-built keyed collection AS A PARAMETER (`fn lookup(h: hash<R[ck]>, k)`) works fine.  Surfaced building the @PLN6-PF2 spatial index — a `build_cell_index() -> hash<…>` helper was unusable, so callers build the index inline instead.  **Root cause (diagnosed 2026-05-21)**: the @P295 reassignment branch in `parse_assign_op` (`src/parser/expressions.rs` ~line 1207) fires for the FIRST assignment too — pass-1 sets `x`'s type to the keyed type (line ~982), so in pass-2 `f_type` is keyed and the branch emits `Insert([OpReplaceKeyed(mk(), x, …)])`, DESTROYING the `Set(x, …)` node.  `compute_intervals` (`src/variables/intervals.rs`) only records a `first_def` from `Value::Set`, so `x` gets none → `assign_slots` skips it → `stack_pos` stays `u16::MAX` → `generate_var` asserts when `len(x)` reads it.  (Struct/vector returns work because they keep a real `Set`.)  **Fixed 2026-05-21** by prepending `Set(var_nr, Null)` to the keyed-assignment branch in `parse_assign_op` (`src/parser/expressions.rs`): `Insert([Set(x, Null), OpReplaceKeyed(...)])`.  This needs NO parse-time first-vs-reassign discriminator — `scan_set` (`src/scopes.rs:539`) already does the right thing: on a FIRST assignment `x` is not yet in scope so the `Set(x, Null)` survives → codegen's keyed-Null arm allocates an empty store (`gen_set_first_keyed_null`) and records the slot; on a REASSIGNMENT `v` is already in scope so `scan_set` elides the redundant `Set(v, Null)`, leaving just `OpReplaceKeyed` (whose `remove_claims` clears `v`'s existing store before `copy_claims`).  One-line parser change, no codegen change; both backends (the native emit `null_named + OpDatabase + OpReplaceKeyed` for first-assign, bare `OpReplaceKeyed` for reassign).  Guards: `p300_*` in `tests/issues.rs`, `tests/scripts/120-keyed-return-assign.loft` (cross-mode), `tests/leak_cases/clean/p300_hash_return_assign_no_leak.loft`. | Low (closed) | Fixed — no workaround needed. |

The outer statement parser then saw an unexpected `(` / `[` and fired "Expect token ;" / "Expect token ]" cascades that surfaced to the user even though pass-2 would have resolved everything correctly.  In the viewer's `render_problems_row`, the chain `summary = summary[plen..olen]` followed a `summary = problem_row_summary(rb, id)` call whose helper was defined further down — so `summary`'s type was `Unknown(0)` in pass-1, and the slice parse cascaded.  Inlining the helper exposed the cascade.  Fix: in fields.rs's Unknown-receiver branch, also consume the `(args)` after `.name`, and in `parse_index`'s Unknown-receiver fallback (new), consume `[a..b]` / `[a..]` / `[..b]` / `[..]` ranges.  Runs in BOTH passes so pass-2's "Field of unknown variable" diagnostic doesn't cascade into "Expect token ;" either.  Pinned by `p281_forward_text_returning_fn_method_chain` (the basic case), `p281_forward_fn_slice_on_text_return` (the slice variant — P278's direct shape), `p281_forward_text_returning_fn_method_with_args` + `p281_mutual_forward_text_fns` + `p281_forward_fn_in_println_format` in tests/issues.rs. Status row never updated when the fix landed; closed in audit pass 2026-05-18.  Note: `'\0'` parses correctly BUT `'\0'` IS the character null sentinel by design — casting to integer gives `null`, and `for c in s` over a text containing `'\0'` stops at the NUL. | 281 | **@P281** — **Closed 2026-05-20** — was framed as "two-pass parser doesn't propagate fn return types in pass-1", but the architecture was actually correct.  The two passes ALREADY do the right thing: pass-1 walks bodies loosely for type discovery, pass-2 re-walks with all defs registered and dispatches correctly.  The bug was elsewhere: in pass-1, when a caller's body referenced a forward-defined fn, the call returned `Type::Unknown(0)`, the receiver of any subsequent `.method(args)` / `[a..b]` was Unknown, and `parser/fields.rs::field` / `parse_index`'s Unknown-receiver paths consumed only the method-name token (or one expression for index) — leaving the trailing `(args)` or `[..]` on the stream.  The outer statement parser then fired "Expect token ;" / "Expect token ]" CASCADES that surfaced to the user even though pass-2 would have re-parsed the body cleanly.  Fix: extend the Unknown-receiver token consumption to swallow the full `.name(args)` or `[range]` so no spurious diagnostic escapes pass-1.  Runs in both passes so pass-2's genuine "Field of unknown variable" diagnostic for truly-undefined receivers doesn't cascade either.  Sibling closes @P278 with the same fix (same root cause; P278's symptom was the `[a..b]` slice variant after a self-slice-reassigned text local whose chain involved a forward-fn-result receiver).  Pinned by 5 `p281_*` tests in tests/issues.rs covering the basename_of method-chain case, the multi-arg case, the slice variant, mutual forward refs, and the format-string interp variant. | 283 | **@P283** — Format-string interpolation of a self-slice-reassigned text PARAMETER crashes both backends.  Pattern that triggers it:  (1) fn with 2+ text parameters; (2) self-slice reassignment of one param (`rb = rb[plen..olen];`); (3) format-string return that interpolates BOTH the reassigned param (`{rb}`) AND another param (`{id}`) — `"[{id}] {rb}"`.  Interpreter SIGSEGV'd at op=116 (`OpAppendText`); native failed to compile with rustc E0368 (`var___work_2 += &*(...)` where `var___work_2` is `&mut String`).  **Closed (2026-05-18)** in @PLN42 phase 10.10b.  Root cause: the parser's `assign_text` work-text path (`src/parser/operators.rs:106-113`) constructs `Value::Call(OpAppendText, …)` / `OpClearText` / `OpFormatText` IR for self-referencing text assignments inside a text-returning function.  `text_return` (`src/parser/control.rs:2919`) then promotes the work-text local to a `RefVar(Text)` hidden argument so the buffer is passed in by reference.  But the operator-call codegen path on BOTH backends emitted the local-String variant of these ops — interpreter's `append_text` (`src/state/text.rs:78`) called `string_mut` on what was actually a refvar DbRef slot (treating its 12 bytes as `String` → SIGSEGV); native's `append_text` (`src/generation/text.rs:139`) emitted `var += &*(…)` on a `&mut String` (E0368).  Fix mirrored the existing B7 dispatch (`OpAppendCharacter` → `OpAppendStackCharacter` for RefVar targets) to cover the full op cluster: when the first arg is `Value::Var(v)` with `Type::RefVar(Type::Text(_))`, rewrite to the Stack variant.  Added in `src/state/codegen.rs::generate_call` (bytecode emit — interp dispatch) AND `src/generation/dispatch.rs::output_call_inner` (native emit dispatch).  Op set: `OpAppendText` → `OpAppendStackText`, `OpClearText` → `OpClearStackText`, `OpAppendCharacter` → `OpAppendStackCharacter`, `OpFormatText` → `OpFormatStackText`, `OpFormatInt` → `OpFormatStackInt`, `OpFormatFloat` → `OpFormatStackFloat`, `OpFormatSingle` → `OpFormatStackSingle`, `OpFormatDatabase` → `OpFormatStackDatabase`.  Native side ALSO needed a `refvar_text_clone` arm in `output_set` (`src/generation/dispatch.rs:319`) for the `OpSet(local_String, Var(refvar_text_arg))` shape — `output_code_inner` emits `&*var_X` for RefVar(Text) Var reads (`src/generation/emit.rs:141`); appending `.to_string()` parses as `&*(var_X.to_string())` per Rust precedence → `&str` not `String` → E0308.  Fix: emit `var_X.to_string()` directly (auto-deref through `&mut String` produces an owned String of the right type).  Pinned by `tests/scripts/111-format-string-self-slice.loft` (three cells: prefix-stripped, no-prefix, two-calls-share-buffer). | 289 | **@P289** — **Closed 2026-07-09 via @PLN47.** Canonical object serialization for binary files.  **Shipped**: `f += my_struct` / `s = f#read as MyStruct` round-trips for plain scalar-field structs and nested plain structs (allocated via `OpDatabase` before `OpReadFile` fills fields).  `f#read(N) as vector<T>` reads `N` **bytes** (not elements); parse-time warning when literal `N` is not a multiple of the element width.  `f += ch` / `f#read as character` round-trips as 4 bytes; `f += b` / `f#read as boolean` as 1 byte.  Signed narrow ints (`i8`/`i16`) sign-extend correctly on both backends.  Structs with `text`/`vector`/collection fields are **rejected at compile time** with `read_file:`/`write_file:` diagnostics (nested-struct fields reported as `outer.inner`).  `f += text`/`f += vector<T>` still write raw bytes with no length prefix (caller manages count for GLB-style formats).  **Retained caveat (@P293)**: u32/i32 values ≥ 2³¹ read back as negative i64.  **Known limitation (separate open issue)**: `q = f#read as Struct` leaks one record per read — pre-existing general ownership bug (`x = { …; struct_temp }` leaks in the inline-block shape regardless of file I/O).  Regression harness: `tests/binary_io_matrix.rs` (32 cross-mode cells).  **Historical**: earlier the fix at 2026-05-20 landed append semantics (`f += value` now appends; `f#next = N` overwrites; `f.set_file_size(0)` truncates) + `file.sync()` — also tracked here; see git and CHANGELOG 0.8.6.  Full design in [STDLIB_FILES.md § Struct and vector binary round-trip](STDLIB_FILES.md#struct-and-vector-binary-round-trip-pln47--shipped-2026-07-09) and [plans/47-binary-io-validation/](plans/47-binary-io-validation/README.md). | High (closed) | n/a — struct fields now serialized by the runtime; write/read each field individually only needed for variable-width-field structs or pre-existing code. |
| 294 | **@P294** — **Closed 2026-05-20** (root-caused via `LOFT_STORES=log` + valgrind) — interpreter value-stack buffer overflow.  The interpreter's value stack is store #0, allocated ONCE in `src/state/mod.rs::State::new` via `database(1000)` = 1000 words / 8000 bytes and it NEVER grew afterward.  `reserve_frame` (bare `self.stack_pos += size`), `fn_call`, and `put_stack` all advanced `State.stack_pos` with NO buffer-capacity check, so once cumulative `stack_pos` crossed ~8000 bytes the stack writes (`State::init_text`, `put_stack`, `copy_block`, the coroutine-resume append, native-lib result pushes) wrote PAST the store-#0 buffer.  The guard that should have caught it — the bounds `debug_assert!` in `Store::addr_mut` — is compiled OUT in the release loft library (`[profile.*.package.loft] debug-assertions = false`), so the OOB write silently corrupted the adjacent malloc chunk header; glibc only detected it at program teardown when `Vec<Store>::drop` ran the real `A.dealloc`, producing the "double free or corruption (!prev)" SIGABRT (crash site varied — heap-metadata corruption).  `MAX_CALL_DEPTH = 500` guards recursion COUNT, not stack BYTES — ~40 modest frames already exceed 8000 bytes.  `--native` was unaffected (native codegen uses the real Rust call stack).  Originally mis-attributed to markdown / a teardown free-order bug; the markdown renderer just recurses deep enough to trip it.  **Fix**: new `State::ensure_stack(extra)` grows store #0 in place via `Store::grow_words` → `resize_store` (safe across realloc — `stack_cur` is a `(store_nr, rec, pos)` DbRef, not a raw ptr; the buffer is one record, never relocated).  A cached `State.stack_cap_bytes` keeps the hot push/reserve paths to a single comparison.  Called from `reserve_frame` (frame entry), `put_stack` (value pushes), the coroutine-resume frame copy, and before each native-lib call (1 KiB headroom).  Pinned by `tests/scripts/117-deep-stack.loft` (recurse 250 deep with locals — was SIGSEGV, now valgrind-clean on both backends); the markdown test (`lib/markdown/tests/01-render.loft`) now exits 0 under `--interpret` and is valgrind-clean. | High (closed) | n/a — fix lands in `src/state/mod.rs` (`ensure_stack` + `stack_cap_bytes` field + calls at the 4 stack-advancing sites) + `src/store.rs` (`grow_words` public wrapper over `resize_store`). |
| 295 | **@P295** — **Closed 2026-05-20** — Reassigning a KEYED-collection LOCAL (`sorted`/`hash`/`index`) from another keyed local — `s = ns` — panicked in codegen (`Unknown var s type sorted<…>` at `src/state/codegen.rs:2896`): `gen_put_var` has `OpPutRef` arms for `Vector`/`Reference`/`Enum`/`Iterator`/`Tuple` but NONE for keyed kinds → `_ => panic!`.  Sibling of @P292 (vector, closed 2026-05-19); even adding an `OpPutRef` arm would ALIAS the RHS store and dangle when a loop-local RHS is freed.  **Fix**: new deep-copy op `OpReplaceKeyed(src, dest, tp)` (`src/state/io.rs::replace_keyed` interp + `src/codegen_runtime.rs::OpReplaceKeyed` native + dispatch arm) doing `remove_claims(dest)` + `copy_claims(src, dest, tp)` — NO `copy_block` (a keyed local's slot is a `DbRef` to a dedicated store; the collection header is at `(store,1,8)`, so block-copying `size(tp)` bytes corrupts it).  `copy_claims` rebuilds the per-kind index (`copy_claims_seq_vector`/`_hash_body`/`_index_body`).  Parser emit in `parse_assign_op` computes the SPECIFIC keyed type id (via `database.sorted/hash/index(c,key)`, NOT the generic-alias `known_type` = u16::MAX) and `0x8000`-tags fresh-storage call RHS for source-free.  CRITICAL second half: strip the `s["ns"]` lifetime dep the assignment sets up (extended `Variables::make_independent` to handle keyed kinds) — without it, scope analysis treats `s` as borrowing `ns`, suppressing `s`'s own `OpFreeRef` (store leak) and deferring `ns`'s free (loop accumulation / element doubling).  Closing the hash case also surfaced + fixed TWO pre-existing latent bugs in the hash deep-copy machinery (masked until now because hash deep-copy ran only on clean/fresh dest memory; the reassignment's `remove_claims`-then-reclaim path reuses dirty memory and exposed them): (1) a **@P290-sibling off-by-one** in `remove_claims`'s hash arm (`room*2` → `(room-1)*2`; the old bound walked 2 bucket slots past the allocation → `delete()` on garbage → SIGSEGV); (2) `copy_claims_hash_body` claimed `size.div_ceil(8)` words (missing the +1 header word) and `copy_block`'d `size - 4` bytes from offset 4, UNDER-copying the element struct (which lives at offset 8, `size` bytes) and leaving the last field's bytes as stale reclaimed memory (nondeterministic garbage in an i64 field's high bits) — fixed to match the proven `copy_claims_index_body` (claim `1 + size.div_ceil(8)`, set back-pointer, `copy_block` full `size` from offset 8).  Pinned by `p295_sorted_reassign_from_loop_local` + `p295_hash_index_reassign` (tests/issues.rs interp) + `p295_keyed_reassign_no_leak` + `p295_keyed_reassign_call_rhs_no_leak` (tests/leak.rs) + `tests/scripts/119-keyed-local-reassign.loft` (BOTH backends).  Works on interp AND `--native` — the native half was initially blocked by the pre-existing keyed-LOCAL native bug [@P296](#open-issues--quick-reference), fixed in the same session.  `spacial` reassignment stays rejected with a diagnostic (`copy_claims`/`insert_record` don't implement Spacial). | High (closed) | n/a — fix lands in `src/state/io.rs`, `src/codegen_runtime.rs`, `src/generation/dispatch.rs`, `src/parser/expressions.rs`, `src/variables/mod.rs`, `src/database/allocation.rs` (hash off-by-one), `default/01_code.loft` (op decl), `src/fill.rs` (regen). |

Root cause #1: `default/01_code.loft`'s `u32` typedef was `integer limit(0, 4294967294)` with NO `size(4)` annotation, so `forced_size(u32_alias)` returned None and the parser's `f#read as u32` cast path fell through to `self.get_type(&tp)` (resolves to the bare integer/i64 type, 8-byte read).  i32 worked because its typedef has `size(4)`.  Surfaced 2026-05-19 in the @PLN6 phase 1.4 tick-loop work where the persistence header had magic + version + tick + count as `u32` fields.  Fix #1: add `size(4)` to u32 in default/01_code.loft.  But — adding size(4) routed u32 fields through `database.int(min, false)` → Parts::Int storage (4-byte signed), which exposed **three latent narrow-key hash bugs**: **Root cause #2**: `src/database/types.rs::determine_keys` mapped any `fld.content > 5` to `Key::type_nr = 7` (the catch-all), so hash<Row[i32_field]> (and the new u32 case) lost their type-width information.  Fix #2: new helper `key_type_nr_for_content` that inspects `self.types[content].parts` and routes Parts::Int → 8, Parts::Short → 9, Parts::Byte → 10, Parts::ShortRaw → 11; non-integer custom types still go to 7.  **Root cause #3**: `src/state/io.rs::read_key`'s catch-all `_ => key.push(Content::Long(i64::from(*self.get_stack::<u8>())))` popped 1 byte while the lookup value was always pushed as i64 (8 bytes) — so for any non-built-in key type the lookup ran against `Content::Long(0)` instead of the actual key.  Fix #3: when the content's Parts is Int/Short/ShortRaw/Byte, pop the full i64 from the stack.  **Root cause #4**: `src/keys.rs::hash_ref` / `compare_key` / `compare_ref` / `get_key` only had arms for type_nr 1..6; new type_nrs 8..11 added with the right per-storage read (get_i32_raw, get_short, get_byte, raw u16 → i16 sign-extend).  All four arms produce Content::Long(i64) so the hash matches across insert and lookup.  Net effect: a `hash<Row[i32_field]>` or `hash<Row[u32_field]>` now does correct key lookup; an `f#read as u32` reads 4 bytes; a `f += val as u32` writes 4 bytes (with the same i32-signed-range caveat as `as i32` — values 2^31..2^32-1 round-trip via raw bytes but read back as negative i64s in expressions).  Pinned by `p184_hash_sorted_narrow_key_field` (existing, now exercises the Parts::Int path) and new `p293_narrow_key_hash_lookup` in tests/issues.rs.  Sibling site to watch: any future Parts variant added to storage needs a matching arm in determine_keys + the four keys.rs callsites + read_key. | High (closed) | n/a — fix lands in `default/01_code.loft` (u32 typedef gets size(4)), `src/database/types.rs::determine_keys` (key_type_nr_for_content helper), `src/state/io.rs::read_key` (i64 pop for narrow ints), `src/keys.rs` (4 callsites: hash_ref / compare_key / compare_ref / get_key gain arms 8..11). |

| 290 | **@P290** — **Closed 2026-05-19** — Two cooperating bugs presented as one.  Symptom: `fn snap(w: World) { v: vector<integer> = []; for cell in w.cells { v += [cell.x]; } }` (or any callee iterating a passed-in struct's hash field) panicked in `src/store.rs:250` with `Claim on locked store (size=N) (locked by: lock_store(store_nr=M, rec=1))`.  **Root cause #1**: `src/state/codegen.rs`'s fn-call deep-copy bracket locks every Ref/Vector/Enum arg around the call so `OpCopyRecord`'s `0x8000` free-source bit can't accidentally free a caller's arg if the callee returned a borrowed view.  The lock's intent was "don't free me" but its side effect was "block all writes/claims" — which crashed `build_hash_sorted_vec` (`src/database/allocation.rs:437`) when its C60-mandated in-store scratch claim hit the locked store.  **Root cause #2** (sibling SEGV, only exposed if you skip the lock): `src/database/allocation.rs::copy_claims_hash_body` looped `1..length*2` to walk hash buckets, but `length` here is actually `room` (per `src/hash.rs::add` layout: offset 0 = room/words, offset 4 = entry count, offset 8+ = (room-1)×2 bucket slots).  The wrong bound BOTH skipped the first bucket (i starts at 1, not 0) AND walked 2 indices past the destination claim's end (writes offsets 72 + 76 of a 72-byte allocation), corrupting heap metadata → SIGSEGV on the next allocator op.  Only triggered by SRet deep-copy of a struct containing a populated hash — exactly `fn collect(w: World) -> ChunkSet { … cs.chunks += [...]; cs }`.  Fix #1 + cleanup: split the single `Store.locked` flag into TWO orthogonal flags — `Store.read_only` (hard immutability: CONST_STORE / worker borrow / JSON null sentinel) blocks `claim` / `addr_mut` / `delete`; new `Store.free_protected` (soft "don't-free" marker set by the fn-call deep-copy bracket via `Stores::lock_store` → `Store::set_free_protected`) blocks ONLY `delete` and `OpCopyRecord`'s source-free.  The lock-origin-string discriminator hack was removed (cleaner type-level separation).  Fix #2: corrected the loop in `copy_claims_hash_body` to `for i in 0..elms` where `elms = (room - 1) * 2` matches `hash::add`'s layout.  Pinned by `p290_iter_struct_hash_field_no_panic` + `p290_sret_struct_with_hash_field_deep_copy_no_crash` in `tests/leak.rs`; the rename also touched the should_panic test in `tests/expressions.rs::claim_on_locked_store_panics` (now expects "Claim on read-only store").  Projector cleanup: `tools/audience-demo/projector.loft::main` reverted from the inline-snapshot workaround back to a clean `snapshot_cells(world: World) -> CellSnap` helper.  **Open follow-up**: `free_protected` is currently a safety net for `is_borrowed_view` mis-detection in `codegen.rs:1357`; once dep inference is provably complete (audit pass: match-arm union deps, lambda-capture deps, transitive borrow propagation), the entire call-bracket + `free_protected` machinery can be retired. | High (closed) | n/a — fix lands in `src/store.rs` (split flags + `set_free_protected` / `clear_free_protected` setters) + `src/database/allocation.rs::copy_claims_hash_body` (loop bounds) + `src/database/allocation.rs::lock_store` (route to free_protected) + `src/codegen_runtime.rs::copy_record` + `src/state/io.rs::copy_record` (free-source check now `!read_only && !free_protected`). |

| 319 | **@P319** — **FIXED 2026-05-23** — INTERPRETER-ONLY (native was always correct): a lambda stored in a local that CAPTURES a struct and APPENDS a nested-constructed element to the captured struct's `vector<…>` field corrupted the captured struct on the SECOND invocation.  Surfaced at `lib/moros_render/tests/adversarial.loft` (`m_adv3.vertices += [vertex(vec3(x,y,z), up_adv, uv_adv)]` inside a captured lambda → SIGSEGV op=196 `new_record`).  Offset-0 vector field → silent data-loss (`len` stuck at 0: 1st append `inside len=1`, 2nd `inside len=0`); non-zero-offset field → SIGSEGV.  **TRUE ROOT CAUSE** (the earlier "(`copy_claims`/`record_new` store-recycle)" hypothesis was DISPROVEN — `copy_claims` is clean for inline-nested structs, and the same nested append INLINE works): the **void-return closure write-back** (`src/parser/control.rs`, the post-call capture reload) emitted `outer = OpGetDbRef(closure, off)` for EVERY capture — including shared-reference captures stored via the auto-Reference 12-byte DbRef encoding (closure attribute `Reference(d, deps)`, non-empty deps).  For these the interpreter's ref-reassignment freed the OLD store BEFORE writing the SAME DbRef back over itself (`FreeRef(old m)` then `GetDbRef`): a premature free of a store the closure record still referenced (`m`'s `ref_count` is 1 — captured structs got NO `OpIncRc`, unlike boxed `__cell_*` scalars).  The next invocation reused the freed store for its temps, clobbering the captured struct.  Native compiles the same IR without that free, so only the interpreter corrupted.  **FIX:** generalise the write-back skip (previously `__cell_*`-only) to ALL shared-reference captures (`matches!(attr_type, Type::Reference(_, deps) if !deps.is_empty())`) — the closure already shares the live store, so the reload was redundant as well as harmful.  Validated: probe `1/2/2`, `moros_render` adversarial 9/9 + full 155, `moros_map` 51, `moros_editor` 44, `moros_sim` 137, `moros_ui` 41 — interp AND native; `moros_*` removed from `LIB_PKGS_SKIP`; the "(2) `moros_map/types.loft:32`" site was indeed an in-process artifact (passes in isolation).  Regression: `tests/scripts/repro_p319.loft` (offset-0 + non-zero-offset variants, both backends via `loft_suite` + `native_scripts`) and the un-gated `test_adv_lambda_mutates_mesh`. | ~~Medium (interp crash / silent data-loss)~~ FIXED | n/a (fixed) — historical workaround was to build the nested element OUTSIDE the closure (assign to a local, then append it), or run under `--native`. |

Keyed INSERT (`h.data += […]` / `h.data[key] = Ent{…}`) and whole-field reassign (`h.data = []`) WERE recognized.  **ROOT CAUSE:** `coll[key] = null` lowers (via `collections.rs::towards_set_hash_remove`) to `OpHashRemove(OpGetField(h,…), …)` — the base var nested in arg[0], exactly like the working `OpSetKeyed`/`OpClearKeyed` shapes — but the `first_arg_write` op-name set consulted by `find_written_vars` (`src/parser/mod.rs`) listed `OpSetKeyed`+`OpClearKeyed` and NOT `OpHashRemove`, so `collect_vars_in(arg[0])` never ran and `h` was never marked written. Validated: repro compiles + runs (entry removed, mutation propagated through the `&`) on interp AND native; regression `tests/scripts/repro_p320.loft` (hash + index variants, both backends). Plan `lib_plans/12-library-extraction` (promoted from `future/` to active 2026-05-23) is the correct vehicle — Phase 1 drains `src/native.rs` of library symbols, then `lib/imaging/native/` moves to a real external crate consumable via the package registry.  @P321c stays open until the imaging library's `n_load_png` resolves via the package's own native crate (no compiler-crate routing).  Direct-call ABI gap (lack of struct-ref marshalling) is a real native-codegen limitation but its fix lives downstream of the extraction work, not in the compiler crate.  **@P321c native dimension — FIXED 2026-05-25**: the direct-call ABI gap is now closed in the compiler's GENERIC native codegen (no library code added to the compiler crate — the cdylib body stays in `lib/imaging/native/`).  `output_native_direct_call` (`src/generation/mod.rs`) now (1) forwards a `LoftStore` whenever a `#native` fn takes a struct `Reference` ARG — built from that arg's own `store_nr`, not the null store, mirroring the interpreter's `make_loft_store(stores, first_ref_store(args))` — and (2) marshals each `Reference` arg as a `LoftRef` via `loft::codegen_runtime::to_loft_ref` + `transmute_copy` (no `loft_ffi` type named, dodging the dual-crate StableCrateId collision), so a store-MUTATING package fn like `load_png(path, image)` gets its full 4-arg ABI `(store, path_ptr, path_len, image)`.  Two companion bugs fixed alongside: (i) `loft generate`'s `*_fields` offsets were computed by a SEPARATE layout match (plain `integer` treated as 4 bytes) that DIVERGED from the runtime — now read from the canonical schema (`Stores::position`/`Stores::size`), giving the real layout `width@0`/`height@8`/`name@16`/`data@20` (record 24 bytes) for `Image`; (ii) `lib/imaging/native/src/lib.rs` had hardcoded the OLD wrong offsets with `set_int` (i32) — corrected to the canonical offsets and to `set_long`/`get_long` for the i64 `width`/`height`.  imaging un-skipped from `LIB_PKGS_NATIVE_SKIP`; `tests/native.rs::native_library_suite` now 53 green incl. imaging, and the interpreter (which dlopens the same cdylib) reads `width=256 name=map.png len=65536`. The browser path borrows the platform's image codec exactly as the design called for: `--html` auto-discovers `*.png` siblings of the entry `.loft` and base64-embeds each into `ctrl.assets[basename]` (`src/main.rs`); the HTML preamble decodes via `createImageBitmap` + `OffscreenCanvas.getImageData` (`doc/loft-gl-wasm.js::decodeLoftAssets`) BEFORE `loft_start()` so the wasm-side lookup is synchronous; the `n_load_png` codegen body routes to `loft_imaging_wasm::imaging_load_png` (lookup populated from `lib/imaging/loft.toml::[wasm.bridge.routes]` into `Data::wasm_bridge_routes`, read by `src/generation/mod.rs::output_native_direct_call`) which calls three `loft_gl` host imports (`imaging_query`, `imaging_copy_rgb`, `imaging_save`) defined in `lib/imaging/wasm/host.js` and writes width/height/name + an allocated pixel vector (`loft::vector::alloc_vector_from_bytes`) into the Image struct.  `save_png` triggers a browser download via `OffscreenCanvas.convertToBlob` + `URL.createObjectURL` + `<a download>`. The `&`-ref arg `st.es_tools` (an addressable field) materialises a `Set(__ref_N, OpGetField…)` statement ahead of the call, so the RHS is `Insert([Set(__ref_N, …), Call])` — wrapped in `Value::Span` for source-position tracking.  `output_set`'s S35 hoist (`src/generation/dispatch.rs`) — which exists to lift all-but-last Insert ops to statements — matched only a *bare* `Insert`, so `Span(Insert)` fell through to the brace-less `Insert` arm of `output_code_inner`, producing `let x = let __ref_N = …; call()`. | 233 | **@P233** — `tests/testing.rs::code!()` test harness hung the loft parser/lexer when the expected `Value::Text` value contained JSON-escape sequences (`\"`, `\\`). Order matters — the later steps add fresh `\` characters as part of canonical escape sequences (`\n`, `\"`) that should NOT be re-escaped. | 220 | **@P220** — `""` literals stored into a `vector<text>` then deep-copied (struct field assignment, `vector_add`, `OpCopyRecord`, parallel-worker boundary, etc.) silently re-classified as `null` on the destination.  Both backends agreed on the wrong output, so the bug was in shared code: `Stores::copy_claims` in `src/database/allocation.rs` checked the *string content* via `s.is_empty()` to decide whether to allocate or write the null sentinel.  `get_str(0)` returns `STRING_NULL` (`"\0"`, len 1) for a null source, so the empty-content check never fired for genuine nulls — but it DID fire for genuinely empty `""` sources, writing 0 (the null sentinel) and re-classifying the value as null.  **Closed (2026-05-05)** by discriminating on the source `cur` field (non-zero = allocated, regardless of string length): nulls stay null, `""` round-trips as `""`. | 236 | **@P236** — A function whose return type is a heap-owned reference (`Reference`, `Vector`, struct-enum) and whose body's tail expression is an `if/else` (or `match`) returning newly-constructed records corrupted the return value on `--native` (returned the typed null sentinel `DbRef { store_nr: u16::MAX, rec: 0, pos: 8 }` instead of the if/else's value).  Interpreter worked because `OpReturn(value=12)` reads from the eval-stack top (which the if/else branches populate); native generated `if cond { …; w } else { …; w }; OpFreeRef(w); return DbRef::null();` and the if/else's value was dropped on the floor.  Reproducer: `fn make_p(c: bool) -> P { if c { P{x:1, y:2} } else { P{x:3, y:4} } }` — interp printed `(1, 2)`, native printed `(null, null)`.  Verified pre-existing on `eebaaa4`.  Surfaced 2026-05-08 during ARC.md A7.1 (par tuple wide-return) when widening the parse_function gate to route pure-value tuple returns through `Reference(__tuple<…>)` exposed the same shape — `min_max(...) -> (integer, integer) { if cond { (a, b) } else { (b, a) } }` regressed `tests/docs/28-tuples.loft`.  **Closed (2026-05-08)** by three coordinated changes: (a) `parser/control.rs::unify_if_branches_work_refs` walks the body's tail If/Match (else-if chains lower to nested `Value::If`) and rewrites the false branch's terminal work-ref `Var` references via the new `substitute_work_ref` helper so both branches end with the SAME `Var(shared_w)`; gated to fire only when both terminal vars match `__ref_*` / `__rref_*` naming so user-named parameters are never renamed (regression caught when `gen_max<T: Ordered>(gen_x: T, gen_y: T)` accidentally returned `gen_x` for both branches).  (b) `scopes.rs::returned_var` recurses through `Value::If` and reports the shared var when both branches match, so `get_free_vars` skips OpFreeRef on it; `ref_return` then promotes it to a hidden caller arg as for single-branch reference returns.  (c) `scopes.rs::free_vars` catchall emits `Return(Var(ret_var))` instead of the legacy `Return(Null)` (which depended on the bytecode VM's eval-stack-top semantics — broken under native).  Same path closes the tuple variant: `parser/control.rs::rewrite_tail_tuple_to_synthetic_struct` recurses through If/Block/Insert with a SHARED work-ref via `rewrite_tail_tuple_with_work_ref`, so the synthetic-struct construction sequence reuses one DbRef across all branches.  Pinned by `tests/issues.rs::p236_struct_return_from_if_else_{true,false}` + `_else_if_chain` + `_param_returning_if_unaffected` (regression guard for the parameter-substitution gate) + `_tuple_return_from_if_else` + the previously-blocked `tests/threading_chars::par_tuple_return_{int_int,three_arity,nested}` canaries (un-ignored).  Side benefit: closes ARC.md A7.1 entirely. | High (closed) | n/a — fix lands in `src/parser/control.rs` (unify helper + tuple recursive rewrite + tail_has_tuple_leaf gate), `src/scopes.rs` (returned_var recursion + Return-with-ret-var emission), `src/parser/definitions.rs` (size-based tuple_rewrite gate widen), `src/parser/expressions.rs` (Reference(__tuple<...>) destructure arm). |

| 240 | **@P240** — A bounded-generic function whose body computes TWO OR MORE bound-supplied operator results into local variables, then returns them in a tuple constructor, produced wrong values under one backend in a way that depended on whether the body had intervening side-effects (e.g., a `println!`). When `a` and `b` were both written in the same scope, the second write clobbered the first; the tuple read returned `(b_value, b_value)`.  Fix: add `Value::Tuple(elems)`, `Value::TupleGet(_, _)`, and `Value::TuplePut(_, _, _)` arms that recurse into each element so reads update the operand vars' last_use correctly.  **(b) Native hoisted-return guard miss**: `scopes::free_vars` hoists the tuple value as a separate statement before the `OpFreeText(work)` cleanup, leaving `[Set(lt), Set(gt), Call(println), Tuple, OpFreeText, Return(Null)]`.  `src/generation/emit.rs::patch_hoisted_returns` already rewrites this to `Return(Tuple)` — but only when the block result type matches a small allow-list (Void / Never / `t_*` text-stub / `__ret_N` text temp).  T-stubs returning a tuple weren't in the list, so native emitted the tuple as a discarded statement and fell through to a hardcoded `return (0, 0)`.  Fix: add `is_t_stub_tuple_body` to the guard so tuple-returning T-stubs also run the patch.  Without (a) the no-side-effects shape was wrong on interp; without (b) the with-println shape was wrong on native.  Both shapes now correct on both backends.
